# IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md

Generated 2026-10-27 from `specs/` (regenerated by hand the same day after the specs absorbed the planner's open questions; see specs/00 §Decisions). Specs are the source of truth; this file
is disposable. State at generation: specs written, no `lib/`, `test/`,
`bench/` or `mix.exs`, no commits on the branch. Every milestone starts from
zero.

Conventions:

- One task = one commit = one build-loop iteration, under an hour.
- Tasks are ordered so that every dependency sits above its dependents. The
  build loop takes the topmost unchecked task.
- Tasks marked **(hot path)** touch `Theine.get/2`, `Theine.put/4`,
  `Theine.ReadBuffer` or `Theine.Entry` and must run `mix bench get` and paste
  the summary table into the commit body (AGENTS.md).
- Tasks marked **(maintenance)** must run `mix bench maintenance`; tasks marked
  **(policy)** must run `mix bench hitrate` (specs/10 §Gates, step 6).
- Stress-tier tests are tagged `:stress` and excluded from `mix test`
  (specs/10 §Tiers).

- [x] M0.4 `mix bench.traces` downloader and synthetic traces (specs/09 §`mix bench hitrate` traces table, §Decisions 2026-10-27 M0.4): manifest `bench/traces.exs`, `:httpc`/`file://` download with `:none | :gzip | :zstd` decompression, SHA-256 check before rename, `loop` and `zipf` generated from a fixed seed with recorded hashes — outcome met for the synthetic traces (generated, verified, re-run is a no-op) and for the download path against `file://` fixtures (plain, gzip, zstd verified; mismatch, missing hash and missing URL each exit non-zero naming the trace). Split: the ARC URLs and hashes are M0.4b.

Mirror: `moka-rs/cache-trace` (the ARC source Caffeine's simulator wiki links to), zstd files. The first real download exposed a bug in M0.4's downloader: Mix prunes the `:public_key` code path, so `:httpc.ssl_verify_host_options/1` crashed; fixed with `Mix.ensure_application!/1` for `:inets`, `:ssl`, `:public_key`. Outcome met: `mix bench.traces` exits 0 with all six traces, a second run reports every trace `ok`, decompressed sizes match the independent `djblue/caching` mirror byte for byte, OLTP has the paper's 914,145 lines, and `mix bench hitrate --traces OLTP,P3,S3,DS1` exits 0 with an `lru` row per size. Note for later tasks: the loop's allowlist permits `curl -fsSL`, so downloads from inside the loop work when invoked with exactly those flags.
- [x] M0.5 `mix bench hitrate` replay harness with the reference LRU (specs/09 §`mix bench hitrate`): trace loader for the ARC `.lis` format and the synthetic format, `lru` policy, per-trace/size hit-rate table, JSON result, non-zero exit when a threshold fails — outcome: `mix bench hitrate` runs the `lru` row for every trace and size listed in specs/09 and exits 0 on the empty library. Met for `loop` and `zipf`. The ARC rows fail loudly (one A3 "trace available" threshold per size) until M0.4b records the traces, so `mix bench hitrate` exits non-zero today and `mix bench hitrate --traces loop,zipf` exits 0. The ARC reader was checked against a fixture only.

Met. The write and mixed drivers (`Theine.Bench.Load`) are complete and subject-agnostic. They were checked against a fake ETS subject with a trimming process: the sections render, and a failed overshoot exits non-zero. Load parameters are in specs/09 §Decisions M0.6.

## M1 Store and API without a bound

Done: M1.1, M1.2, M1.3, M1.4, M1.5, M1.6. M1 complete.

- [x] M1.1 Options, `%Theine.Config{}`, `Theine.Owner`, `Theine.Supervisor`, `start_link/1`, `child_spec/1` (specs/08 §Starting a cache, §Supervision tree): option validation with every option and default from the specs/08 table, `max_size: :infinity` only (finite raises `ArgumentError` until M2.4), Owner creates the entries table with the specs/01 options plus the `ids`, `pending`, `flags` atomics and counters, writes config to `persistent_term` once and never again, deletes it in `terminate/2`; `Task.Supervisor` and a `Theine.Maintenance` GenServer registered as `Theine.Maintenance.<name>` (atom; specs/08 §Decisions) with `reset/1`, `run/1`, `info/1` returning its (empty) state; `rest_for_one` — tests: theine_test "starts two caches with different names", "second start of the same name returns already_started", "unknown option raises ArgumentError naming the option", "stopping the cache removes persistent_term, named tables and registered names", "killing Owner restarts Owner, TaskSupervisor and Maintenance in order" (restart order observed with a `:procs` trace session on the supervisor). Met. Also config_test for every option's default and type check, maintenance_test for `reset/1`, `run/1`, `info/1` and the `pending`/drain-flag reset in `init/1`. Notes for M1.2: `config.counters` is a 7-field `:counters` ref with no names yet (M1.6 names them); Maintenance takes only the cache name and reads the config from `persistent_term` in `init/1`; the drain flag index is `Theine.Owner.drain_flag/0`.
- [x] M1.2 **(hot path)** `Theine.Entry` layout, `put/4`, `get/2`, `get/3`, write events by `send`, `size/1`, `weighted_size/1` (specs/01 §Entry layout, §Read path steps 1–3 and 6, §Write path; specs/03 §Write events events and `pending` counter, send path only; specs/08 `size/1`): `Theine.WriteEvents.deliver/2` does `add_get(pending)` then `send` to the registered name; Maintenance applies each `{:put, …}`/`{:removed, …}` on receipt (specs/04 §The pass, first paragraph) to a `Theine.Policy` key table (`policy` `{key, id, segment, seq, weight, expires_at}` and `ids` `{id, key}`, unbounded mode: no order table, no sketch), decrements `pending`, and maintains the `size`/`weighted_size` atomics; `put` with `ttl:` sets `expires_at`, `expire_after_write` is the default — tests: theine_test "put then get from another process returns the value", "get/3 returns the default on a miss", "size/1 counts distinct keys after overwrites once maintenance is idle", "weighted_size/1 follows weight: option"; tracing_test "hit performs exactly persistent_term.get, ets.lookup, counters.add" and "miss performs exactly persistent_term.get, ets.lookup, counters.add" (policy-disabled multisets, specs/08 §Acceptance); `mix bench get` `theine_unbounded` subject added and within A1/A2 thresholds against `ets`. Met (table under §Benchmark log) at the A1/A2 figures reset on 2026-10-27 (specs/00, 09 §Decisions): 30/30 checks at 1.5×/0.65×, where the same tree passed 20/30 at the original 1.3×/0.75×. Notes for later tasks: the sizes live in a 2-field `config.sizes` atomics (read with `Theine.Policy.size/1`, `weighted_size/1`), zeroed by `Policy.new/1` in `Maintenance.init/1`; the policy state is a `%Theine.Policy{}` struct passed first, so the spec's `on_write(key, id, weight, expires_at)` is `Policy.on_write/5` and `on_remove/3` (specs/05 §Decisions); unbounded rows carry `segment: nil`; `Maintenance.policy_row/2` and `info/1`'s `size`/`weighted_size` fields exist for tests; the counter indices (`hits` 1, `misses` 2, `expirations` 6) are module attributes in `Theine.Entry` until M1.6 names them in `Theine.Stats`; `put/4` already supports `weight:` (the weigher and oversized rejection stay in M1.5); the specs/01 §Expired read branch (delete by exact object, `{:removed, key, id}`, `misses` and `expirations`) is implemented and tested with a past `expires_at` written into the table, so M1.4 is reduced to the injected clock and its tests; `Maintenance.run/1` is the way a test waits for its own write events (they queue ahead of the call), `info/1`'s `pending` for events from other processes.
- [x] M1.3 Write-event backpressure and maintenance pause/resume (specs/03 §Delivery with backpressure; specs/04 §Responsibilities `pause/1`, `resume/1`): above `write_hwm` the writer uses `GenServer.call({:event, event}, :infinity)`; Maintenance consumes `{:event, …}` calls and plain events in mailbox order, replying as it consumes; `pause/1`/`resume/1` available only when the cache is started with the test-only `test_hooks: true` option — tests: write_events_test "writers block once pending exceeds write_hwm while maintenance is paused", "mailbox never exceeds write_hwm + schedulers messages", "every blocked writer returns :ok after resume and pending returns to 0". Met. Also maintenance_test for the `{:event, _}` call path, the `ArgumentError` without `test_hooks`, a paused process consuming nothing until `resume/1`, and `resume/1` as a no-op; config_test covers the option. Notes for later tasks: `test_hooks` is a `start_link` option (specs/08 table, specs/04 §Decisions); a paused process blocks in a selective receive, so `info/1` and `run/1` block until `resume/1` and tests read `pending` and `Process.info(pid, :message_queue_len)` from outside while paused (M1.4's "with maintenance paused" and M2.3's "drainer paused" tests can rely on this); the never-crash-the-writer catch around `send` and the call stays in M2.9 as planned, so a writer blocked in the call when Maintenance dies today exits with the call's reason.
- [x] M1.4 **(hot path)** The injected clock for lazy expiry (specs/01 §Expired read; specs/06 §Semantics bullets 1–3, §Clock): the expired-read branch itself shipped in M1.2 (`Theine.Entry.get/2` deletes the exact tuple, sends `{:removed, key, id}`, increments `misses` and `expirations`); this task adds the clock via `Application.compile_env(:theine, :clock, nil)` (specs/06 §Clock) so production compiles to the BIF and test uses `Theine.Test.Clock.now/1` keyed by cache name with a real-clock fallback (`config/config.exs` + `config/test.exs` setting it; `Theine.Entry` reads it at compile time for both `get/2`'s `now` and `put/4`'s `written_at`) — tests: expiry_test "entry with 10 ms TTL is readable at 0 ms and :error at 10 ms with the clock advanced and no pass", "with maintenance paused the reader deletes the expired tuple" (needs M1.3's `pause/1`), "put racing an expired read leaves the new value in place"; tracing_test "expired read performs exactly persistent_term.get, ets.lookup, ets.delete_object, atomics.add_get, counters.add ×2"; `mix bench get` unchanged (the production build still calls the BIF). Met. The clock is the macro `Theine.Clock.now/1` (specs/06 §Decisions), required by `Theine` and `Theine.Entry`, since a macro defined in `Theine.Entry` could not be used by `Entry.put/4` itself and a function call is over the read-path budget; the dev build's abstract code contains the BIF and no reference to the test clock. The race test is both deterministic (the reader's `Entry.expired/2` applied after the put with the stale tuple) and 200 rounds of a real reader/writer race. Note for later tasks: tests that want a controlled clock start `{Theine.Test.Clock, cache: name, at: t}` before writing; caches without one run on real time, so no existing test changed.
- [x] M1.5 `put_new/4`, `delete/2`, `clear/1`, weigher, `weight:` and oversized rejection (specs/01 §Write path `put_new`, `delete`, `clear`; specs/08 §Calls): `delete/2` looks up the current id, deletes by exact object and sends `{:removed, key, id}` (the no-lookup variant is forbidden, specs/01); `clear/1` deletes all objects then calls `Maintenance.reset/1` synchronously; entries with `weight > max_size` are not stored and send no event — tests: theine_test "put_new returns {:error, :exists} and sends no event", "delete is idempotent and removes the policy row", "clear empties the table and the policy tables before returning", "oversized entry is not stored and produces no write event", "weigher result is stored as the entry weight and weight: overrides it". Met. Also: `put_new/4` treats an expired entry as absent (removes it as the expired read does, retries; specs/01 §Decisions) with a clock-driven test; the weigher's result is validated like `weight:`; tracing_test pins the call multisets of `put_new` (new and present key: no `pending` op on the present key, so no event), `delete` (present and absent) and an oversized `put` (no calls at all). Discovered issue 2 is resolved here (specs/05 §Decisions): `Policy.on_remove/3` with a stale id does one `:ets.member/2` on the entries table and drops the row when the key is gone; `%Policy{}` gained a `table` field, policy_test's setup creates the named entries table, and the property generates `:racy_put` events (policy-only writes) alongside table-backed puts and removals; theine_test "concurrent puts of one key followed by delete leave no policy row" runs the real race 200 times. Oversized rejection is tested through `Theine.Entry.put/4` with a bounded copy of a running cache's config, since `start_link` accepts only `:infinity` until M2.4; M2.4 should add the `start_link`-level case. `mix bench get` is a regression check only (the get path is untouched; table under §Benchmark log).
- [x] M1.6 **(hot path)** `Theine.Stats` and `stats: false` (specs/08 §Stats): `:counters` with fields `hits`, `misses`, `loads`, `load_failures`, `evictions`, `expirations`, `refreshes`; `%Theine.Stats{}` with `hit_rate` (0.0 with no requests); `stats: false` skips the counter ops on the unbounded hit and the miss path; `Theine.Stats.read/1` takes `hits` from the counter in unbounded caches and is written so that M2.8 can switch bounded caches to the read-buffer tail sum (specs/03 §Hits from the tails, specs/08 §Stats, human decision 2026-10-27) — tests: stats_test "counts match a scripted sequence of hits, misses and expirations exactly", "hit_rate is 0.0 with no requests"; tracing_test "stats: false hit performs no :counters call"; `mix bench get` `theine_nostats` subject added (unbounded until M2.8). Met. `Theine.Stats` fixes the indices (`hits/0` … `refreshes/0`, `field_count/0` sizes the Owner's counters); `Theine` and `Theine.Entry` read them as compile-time attributes. `Stats.read/1` already dispatches on `config.read_buffer`: `nil` reads the `hits` counter, otherwise `Stats.hits/1` sums the stripe tails from the specs/03 layout (unit-tested with a hand-built array), so M2.8 has nothing to switch; M2.3 must keep that layout or move the sum into `ReadBuffer` and have `Stats` call it. `stats: false` is decided as "request counters off, event counters on" (specs/08 §Decisions M1.6): `get/2` counts neither hits nor misses, the expired-read branch drops only its miss op (tracing_test pins all three `stats: false` multisets), and `expirations` keeps counting. `Theine.stats/1` has a doctest; `clear/1` leaves stats alone (tested). `mix bench get`: `theine_nostats` reported with no threshold (specs/09 applies A1/A2 to `theine` and `theine_unbounded`); table under §Benchmark log.

## M2 Policy

Done: M2.1, M2.2, M2.3, M2.4, M2.5, M2.6, M2.7, M2.8, M2.8b, M2.8c, M2.8d, M2.9, M2.10 (open question 1 resolved 2026-10-29; OLTP is M2.13's), M2.11 (subject and measurement), M2.11c (A5 holds), M2.11d (the pass budget holds; `mix bench maintenance` exits 0), M2.11e (replays deterministic again), M2.11b (floor recorded: the gap is the pass's CPU time on the reader's scheduler; cost per read cut by about a quarter), M2.12 (stress tier; found and fixed phantom policy rows after a restart under writes), M2.13 (adaptive window on by default; `mix bench hitrate` passes every row). M2 complete.

- [x] M2.1 `Theine.Sketch` layout, hashing, `increment/2`, `frequency/2`, `clear/1` (specs/02 §Layout, §Hashing, §Operations minus reset): `words = max(8, next_power_of_two(max_size))` capped at 2^30, `spread/1` mixer, `index/offset` in one private function — tests: sketch_test "layout vectors match the checked-in table", property "frequency equals min(n, 15) after n increments of one key", property "frequency never under-estimates the true count before saturation", "at least 95% of 100_000 single-hit keys report frequency <= 1 at max_size 10_000", "clear/1 zeroes every nibble". Met, with two spec changes (specs/02 §Decisions M2.1): the counter layout is Caffeine's real block layout (`rehash` for the counters, counter `i` in word pair `i` of the block) because the spec's paraphrase reused the block bits for counter 0 and could put two of a key's counters in one nibble; and the collision-quality threshold is now 99% at `frequency <= 3` plus 55% at `<= 1` (measured 99.7% and 61%), since 95% at `<= 1` is impossible at that load (the model and both layouts give about 62%). Notes for later tasks: the sketch is a `%Theine.Sketch{ref, words, block_mask}` struct around an unsigned `:atomics` array (`Config.sketch` typed accordingly; Owner allocates it with `Sketch.new(max_size)` in M2.4); `increment/2` returns `:ok` and does not count, so M2.2's caller keeps `size` and the reset cursor as specs/02 and specs/04 say; `Sketch.dump/1` and `Sketch.positions/2` exist for tests; `clear/1` writes every word (about 1 M atomics ops at `max_size` 1_000_000, fine for `clear/1` and restart, not for a pass).
- [x] M2.2 `Theine.Sketch.reset/1` with chunked aging (specs/02 §Operations `reset/1`, `sample_size`): reset starts when `size >= sample_size`, advances at most `reset_chunk` words per call with a cursor kept by the caller — tests: sketch_test property "after a complete reset every nibble is old >>> 1 and size follows the odd-count rule", "a 2^20-word sketch resets in passes touching at most reset_chunk words each and frequency stays within [old/2, old] throughout". Met. API (specs/02 §Decisions M2.2): `Sketch.reset/1` halves everything and returns `odd`; `Sketch.reset(sketch, cursor, chunk \\ reset_chunk())` advances one chunk from a `{next_word, odd}` cursor (`Sketch.reset_start/0` to begin) and returns `{:more, cursor} | {:done, odd}`; `Sketch.size_after_reset(size, odd)` is the odd-count rule clamped at 0; `Sketch.sample_size/1` is `10 * max_size`. Notes for M2.7: the maintenance state keeps `sketch_size` (increments since the last completed reset, `+1` per `Sketch.increment/2` in `on_write`) and `sketch_reset :: nil | cursor`; start a reset with `reset_start/0` when `sketch_size >= sample_size` and none is running, call `reset/3` once per pass (step 6), and on `{:done, odd}` set `sketch_size = size_after_reset(sketch_size, odd)`; `Theine.clear/1` and a restart call `Sketch.clear/1` and zero both. The default chunk dropped from 65_536 to 16_384 words after measuring the loop at 92 ns per word on a loaded sketch (1.5 ms per chunk; 65_536 words took 6 ms, over the 5 ms `pass_budget` by itself); M2.11 re-measures in a full pass. The test suite grew by about 1 s: the 2^20-word test checks every pass's range and the whole array once.
- [x] M2.3 **(hot path)** `Theine.ReadBuffer` `record/2`, `drain/2`, `schedule_drain/1` (specs/03 §Read buffer): standalone over its own atomics array and flags, not yet wired into `get/2`; `Theine.Stats.hits/1` (M1.6) already sums the stripe tails from the specs/03 layout, so either keep that layout or move the sum into `ReadBuffer.hits/1` and call it from `Stats` — tests: read_buffer_test property "drain yields a sub-multiset of recorded ids, never more than slots per stripe", "drops instead of blocking" (drainer paused, readers at full speed, at most `slots` ids per stripe drained later), "a flood of reads with the drainer paused sends exactly one :drain message", "each stripe receives records with one reader pinned per scheduler". Met. The tail sum moved into `ReadBuffer.hits/1` and `Stats.hits/1` calls it. Two spec decisions (specs/03 §Decisions M2.3): the drain is a fold, `drain(config, acc, fun.(id, acc))` returning `{acc, reads, full?}`, because the policy state must be threaded through `on_access`, with `drain/2` kept for a one-argument function; and `schedule_drain/1` rescues the `ArgumentError` of a send to the unregistered maintenance name during a restart, leaving the flag set. Also `lag/1` (sum of `tail - head`, for `info/1`), `reset_heads/1` (specs/04 §Restart step 2, for M2.9), `stripe_of/2` and `dump/1`. The tests also pin the ring arithmetic by hand (wrap-around, drops with stale ids in the last `slots` sequences, zero slots of readers that claimed but did not write, the threshold crossing at `t - head == drain_threshold`, a full stripe triggering above the threshold) and a second property drains concurrently with the readers. Notes for later tasks: `record/2` matches the config as a plain map (`read_buffer`, `stripes`, `read_buffer_slots`, `drain_threshold`, `flags`, `maintenance`), so M2.8 can call it or inline its body into `get/2` as specs/01 asks; its common path is exactly `system_info, add_get, get, put`; the drain-flag index stays `Theine.Owner.drain_flag/0`; M2.7's pass is `ReadBuffer.drain(config, policy, &Policy.on_access(&2, &1))` after clearing the flag, with `reads` and `full?` feeding telemetry and the follow-up `:drain`. `mix bench get` is a regression check only (`get/2` is untouched; table under §Benchmark log).
- [x] M2.4 **(policy)** `Theine.Policy` bounded mode: regions, `order` table, `on_write/4`, `on_remove/2` (specs/05 §Regions and sizes, §State, §Events `on_write`, `on_remove`): finite `max_size` now accepted by `start_link` (Owner allocates sketch, read buffer and flags only when bounded; `info/1` shows them absent for `:infinity`), new keys placed at window MRU with `Sketch.increment`, updates move to MRU of the current segment, `@max_seq` sentinel asserted — tests: policy_test property "after any sequence of on_write/on_remove every policy key has exactly one order row and one ids row and segment weights equal the sums", "on_remove with a stale id is ignored", "seq never reaches the sentinel" (assertion test), theine_test "max_size: :infinity shows no sketch or read buffer in info/1". Met. The `flags` atomics were already allocated for every cache in M1.1 and stay so (the drain flag costs one word and `Maintenance.init/1` resets it unconditionally); only the sketch and the read buffer are bounded-only. `Policy.limits/2`, `weights/1`, `lru/2`, `mru/2`, `order_rows/1` and `max_seq/0` exist for M2.5/M2.6 and the tests; `sketch_size` lives in the policy struct (specs/05 §Decisions M2.4); `info/1` gained `segments`, `read_buffer` and `sketch` (specs/04 §Decisions M2.4). The bounded property runs the M1.5 event stream against `max_size` 20 and checks the order rows, the window weight and the sketch counts; the sentinel test refuses the write at `max_seq()` with every table untouched. The M1.5 start_link-level oversized-rejection case is in theine_test. Known gap until M2.8: `Theine.stats/1` on a bounded cache reads `hits` from the stripe tails (M1.6 dispatch), which `get/2` does not yet feed, so bounded `hits` reads 0 until M2.8 wires `ReadBuffer.record/2` into `get/2`; the stats_test cases for bounded hits are listed under M2.8 as planned. `mix bench hitrate` run in full (LRU rows only, the `theine` subject is M2.10); table under §Benchmark log.
- [x] M2.5 **(policy)** `Theine.Policy.on_access/1` (specs/05 §Events `on_access`): window → window MRU; probation → protected MRU with demotion of protected LRU entries while `protected_weight > protected_max`; protected → protected MRU; unknown or stale id skipped — tests: policy_test "on_access promotes a probation entry to protected and demotes when over protected_max", "protected_weight <= protected_max after every on_access" (property), "on_access with an old id after the key was rewritten has no effect". Met. `Policy.on_access/2` (state first, specs/05 §Decisions M1.2) looks up `ids` then checks the id against the policy row, moves with the M2.4 `move/5` and counts the key in the sketch; unbounded policies return the state unchanged. One spec addition (specs/05 §Decisions M2.5): a probation entry heavier than `protected_max` is reordered to the probation MRU instead of promoted, Caffeine's `reorderProbation` guard, so the accessed key always ends at the MRU of its new segment and demotion never reaches it. Also tested: a protected access, a demotion of several LRU entries in one call, an entry of exactly `protected_max` promoted, unknown and removed ids, and the sentinel refusal leaving the tables untouched. The new property replays writes, accesses by any issued id, window overflows (the `place/3` helper standing in for M2.6's step 1) and removals against `max_size` 20 and checks the full specs/05 §Acceptance invariant after every event, plus the MRU placement and the sketch count after each current-id access. Note for M2.7: `ReadBuffer.drain(config, policy, &Policy.on_access(&2, &1))` is the whole of step 3. `mix bench hitrate` run in full (LRU rows only, unchanged; the `theine` subject is M2.10); table under §Benchmark log.
- [x] M2.6 **(policy)** `Theine.Policy.evict/1` with W-TinyLFU admission (specs/05 §Events `evict`, §Acceptance): window overflow into probation counted in `candidates`, a candidate cursor walking from probation MRU toward LRU with `:ets.prev/2`, victim vs candidate with `Sketch.frequency/2`, direct victim eviction once `candidates` reaches 0, `:rand` seeded from the cache name in `init/1`, the ≥ 6 random-admit guard, protected/window fallbacks for tiny caches, `evict_batch` cap returning `:more | :done`, removal by re-lookup and id compare (specs/04 §Removing an entry), `evictions` counter — tests: policy_test "victim freq 3 vs candidate freq 5 evicts the victim", "5 vs 3 evicts the candidate", "2 vs 2 evicts the candidate", "after evict returns :done total weight <= max_size", "scan resistance: 800-key working set retains at least 90% after streaming 100_000 distinct keys at max_size 1000", "removal by stale id leaves a concurrently rewritten entry in place". Met. `Policy.evict(state)` returns `{:done | :more, evicted, state}` (specs/05 §Decisions M2.6), `evicted` being table deletions only; the batch cap also counts rows dropped on a stale re-lookup. Step 1 runs on every call, so M2.7's pass calls `evict/1` unconditionally and sends itself `:drain` on `:more`. The carried state is `candidates`, `cursor` (the next candidate's seq, resolved on use to that row or the nearest probation row below it) and `candidate_floor` (the first overflow's seq: the walk never retests entries that were in probation before the batch), all cleared on `:done` and by `reset/1`; a follow-up whose step 1 appended overflows restarts the cursor at the probation MRU. The struct carries `evict_batch` and the stats `counters` from the config, so policy_test's config helper now allocates counters. `Theine.Maintenance.rand_seed/1` is `{h, h, h}` from `phash2(name)` and `init/1` applies it (maintenance_test checks the process dictionary). Tests beyond the plan's list: the per-call window overflow, the 1-in-128 guard (2_000 seeded trials at frequency 6 admit 1–60 times, 500 at 5 admit none), the no-candidate direct eviction, both fallbacks (the window one needs `window_max` hacked above `max_size`, since step 1 otherwise empties it), the cap with carry-over, the cursor restart after new overflows, the floor, `reset/1`, the unbounded no-op, and a 1_000-run property with `evict_batch` 2 that checks the invariant after every event, the window limit after each call and the bound on `:done`. That property found discovered issue 5 (`on_write` left protected over its limit), fixed here. The scan test ages the sketch as the pass will (`Sketch.reset/1` at `sample_size`) and retains exactly 790/800: the 790 promoted keys sit in protected, which eviction never touches while probation has an entry, and the 10 still in the window at scan start overflow one at a time into an empty probation, where each is its own victim. `mix bench hitrate` run in full (LRU rows only, the `theine` subject is M2.10); table under §Benchmark log.
- [x] M2.7 **(maintenance)** The maintenance pass: order, caps, triggers, telemetry (specs/04 §The pass steps 1–3, 5, 6, 8, §Budget; specs/10 §Required helpers `Theine.Test.Policy`): `:drain` handling clears the flag then drains the read buffer into `Policy.on_access/1`, evicts, advances a sketch reset chunk, emits `[:theine, :maintenance, :pass]` with `duration`, `reads`, `writes`, `expired`, `evicted`; triggers: `:drain`, `maintenance_interval` timer, `run/1`, `write_batch` consumed events; step 1 caps at `write_batch * 4`; a full stripe or a capped eviction sends `:drain` to self; `run_maintenance/1` public; instrumented `Theine.Test.Policy` injected via a test-only config field — tests: maintenance_test "call order within one pass is writes, reads, evict", "10_000 queued write events and a full read buffer: one pass consumes at most write_batch * 4 events and stripes * slots reads and schedules a follow-up", "one pass telemetry event with all five measurements", "the interval timer runs a pass on an idle cache". Met. The test-only field is the `start_link` option `policy_module` (default `Theine.Policy`, refused without `test_hooks: true`; specs/08 §Decisions M2.7); `Theine.Test.Policy` in `test/support` forwards to `Theine.Policy` and reports each call to a listener registered with `listen/1`, read back with `calls/1`. Decisions in specs/04 §Decisions M2.7: step 1 is a selective receive of events and `{:event, _}` calls that leaves other messages in place; the `write_batch` trigger is checked on arrival only, so a flush never nests a pass, and a call-path writer is answered before the pass it triggers; the follow-up after a full stripe or a capped eviction goes through `ReadBuffer.schedule_drain/1` so the one-`:drain`-in-flight rule holds; `writes` counts events applied since the previous pass; `expired` is 0 until M3.1; sketch aging keeps its cursor in the maintenance state, shown by `info/1` as `sketch.reset`, and `clear/1` drops it. Also tested: a `:drain` clears the flag and the drained read moves the key (`seq`), eviction in the pass, the `write_batch` trigger on both delivery paths, a capped eviction following up until the cache fits (`evict_batch` 5, 50 evictions over 10 passes), a full stripe scheduling exactly one follow-up, the small-sketch reset in one pass and a two-chunk reset over two passes (the `2 * reset_chunk`-word test takes about 1.2 s). The expiry, write-events and maintenance test setups now start their caches with `maintenance_interval: 60_000` because they count mailbox messages while paused, and the timer would otherwise be a once-per-second flake. `mix bench maintenance` still reports "no theine subject yet" (M2.11); table under §Benchmark log.
- [x] M2.8 **(hot path)** Wire `ReadBuffer.record/2` into `get/2` for bounded caches (specs/01 §Read path step 5; specs/03 §Budget on a hit; specs/09 §`mix bench get`): record only when the cache is bounded; `mix bench get` gains the bounded `theine` subject and `theine_nostats` becomes bounded; the hit-rate and maintenance benches gain their `theine` subjects in M2.10/M2.11 — tests: `stats/1` reports `hits` for a bounded cache as the sum of the stripe tails and the bounded hit does no `:counters` op, with or without `stats: false` (specs/03 §Hits from the tails, specs/08 §Stats) — tests: tracing_test "bounded hit performs exactly persistent_term.get, ets.lookup, atomics.add_get, atomics.get, atomics.put", "bounded hit with stats: false performs the same multiset" and "bounded hit at the drain threshold adds exactly one compare_exchange"; theine_test "reads of one key under a bound keep it resident while 10× distinct keys stream past"; stats_test "bounded hits equal the number of successful gets even when the read buffer drops (maintenance paused)", "a Maintenance restart does not reset bounded hits"; bench: `theine` p50/p99 ≤ 1.5 × `ets`, ips ≥ 0.65 × `ets` at full parallelism (A1, A2; specs/09 §Decisions 2026-10-27). Split on 2026-10-28: the wiring, tests and bench subjects are done here; the A1/A2 figures for the bounded subject are M2.8b and M2.8c below, because the first full run showed two problems that each need a spec decision (discovered issue 6). Done: the hit path reads one config key, the runtime field `hit` (the read-buffer tuple when bounded, else the request counters; specs/08 §Decisions M2.8), after a five-key pattern cost the unbounded hit about 30 ns; the body of `ReadBuffer.record/2` lives in a local `defp record/6` of `Theine` (no call leaves the module, as specs/01 asks), because inlining it into `get/2` made the unbounded hit about 10 ns slower at parallel 4 in the bench than HEAD's small function on the same day (1.43× against 1.36×, `--sizes 1000` with the bounded subjects removed), and a local call costs the bounded hit about 12 ns in a compiled loop (M2.8b rewrites that path anyway). Also measured: the bounded subjects running before the unbounded job in each shape slow the unbounded parallel-4 rows by about 10 ns (1.48× with them, 1.43× without, same tree); the harness order is left alone and the effect is noted here. A bounded `stats: false` cache reports `hits` 0 rather than the tail sum, so the documented "request counters read 0" contract holds in both modes (same decision; tested). Also tested: the bounded miss multiset, the full-stripe drop (add_get, get, compare_exchange, no put, hits still counted), and the scripted stats sequence on a bounded cache. The bench's three theine subjects share one constructor; `theine` and `theine_nostats` are bounded at `max_size: n`. `mix bench get` exits non-zero until M2.8c, as `mix bench hitrate` did between M0.5 and M0.4b; the `theine_unbounded` checks are the gate for this commit (table under §Benchmark log).
- [x] M2.8b **(hot path)** `record/2` in two atomics ops (specs/03 §`record/2`; discovered issue 6): the hit path currently costs about 105 ns over the unbounded hit (measured in a compiled loop: `system_info` 7 ns, each `:atomics` op 18–25 ns, the `schedule_drain/1` call with its two-key match and compare_exchange about 40 ns), and every hit on a full ring takes the compare_exchange, which at full parallelism bounces one cache line between all readers. Proposed design, to be recorded in specs/03 §Decisions: no head read on the hit path, the reader always writes slot `rem(t, slots)` (the ring keeps the most recent `slots` reads rather than the first `slots` after a drain; the drainer already reads only `max(head, tail - slots)..tail - 1`), and the drain trigger is `rem(t, drain_threshold) == 0` so the flag is tested once per `drain_threshold` sequences per stripe instead of on every read above it. Expected cost: `system_info` + `add_get` + `put` ≈ 50 ns over the unbounded hit, within the 1.5× budget at the tightest shape (n=1000 tuple3 parallel=1, where the unbounded subject sits at 1.27–1.37×). Rewrite the ring-arithmetic cases in read_buffer_test and the bounded multisets in tracing_test (hit: add_get, put; every `drain_threshold`-th hit: plus compare_exchange); the properties and the hits-from-tails tests are unchanged. Gate: `theine` and `theine_unbounded` A1 at parallel 1 on every shape. Met, with one addition to the proposed design (specs/03 §Decisions M2.8b, specs/08 options table): the trigger is `rem(t + 1, drain_threshold) == 0` (so a fresh stripe's first read does not schedule a pass of an empty ring), and `stripes`, `read_buffer_slots` and `drain_threshold` must be powers of two, because the two-op protocol with `rem` still measured 77 ns over the unbounded hit in a compiled loop (each `rem` is an integer division, about 11 ns) and 60 ns with masks; the `hit` tuple now carries `{buffer, stripes - 1, 2 + slots, slots - 1, drain_threshold - 1}`, the default `stripes` is `System.schedulers_online()` rounded up (`Config.default_stripes/0`; empty stripes cost the drainer two reads per pass), and a user-supplied size that is not a power of two raises `ArgumentError` naming the option rather than being rounded. The drain side is unchanged (`full?` is still `tail - head >= slots`, the tails still count every hit). The compiled-loop comparison of the three protocols on the same cache (`record` alone, maintenance running): head-reading 165–170 ns, two-op with `rem` 99–111 ns, two-op with masks 65–76 ns. Tests: read_buffer_test rewrites the ring cases (overwrite of the oldest reads, the trigger on every `drain_threshold`-th sequence, a threshold above `slots`, a trigger lost to a set flag retried `drain_threshold` reads later) and its properties generate power-of-two slot counts; tracing_test pins the bounded hit at add_get + put, the trigger hit at + compare_exchange, and a hit past a lapped ring at add_get + put with no head read; config_test covers the power-of-two validation and the rounded default; maintenance_test's follow-up test uses `drain_threshold: 8`. Gate, measured (`mix bench get` table and the latency distribution under §Benchmark log): the bounded p50 at parallel 1 is under 1.5× on four of six shapes and at 1.55/1.58× on the two n=100000 shapes, inside the `ets`-floor wander of discovered issue 4; the bounded p99 at parallel 1 fails on every shape, and the distribution shows that is the pass frequency (p99 is 70–140 ns over p50 with no passes running, 250–360 ns with them), not the hit path, so that part of the gate belongs to M2.8c's duty-cycle cap and is listed there. `mix bench get` still exits non-zero (36/60).
- [x] M2.8c **(maintenance)** Cap the drain-pass duty cycle (specs/04 §The pass triggers; discovered issue 6): under a single saturating reader the maintenance process is busy 98% of wall time (telemetry sum over a 2 M-read run: 7_100 passes, 800 ms of 816 ms, about 110 µs per pass for 64 reads, so 1.7 µs per `on_access`), because a pass that drained a full stripe follows up immediately and readers re-trigger as soon as the flag clears. At full parallelism on the 4-scheduler reference machine that is one core taken from the readers, which caps `theine` ips at about 0.75 × ets before any hot-path cost and fails A2 (measured 0.39–0.52). Proposed rule, to be recorded in specs/04 §Decisions: after a drain-triggered pass of duration `d`, the next `:drain`-triggered pass (reader or follow-up) starts no earlier than `k × d` later (`k` = 3, duty ≤ 25%), implemented by deferring the one in-flight `:drain` with `Process.send_after/3` while the flag stays set so readers send nothing more; `run/1`, the interval timer and the `write_batch` trigger are not deferred, and a deferred pass still runs every step. Reads during the deferral are dropped by the ring, which specs/03 allows (the policy needs a sample). Tests: maintenance_test "drain-triggered passes under a flooding reader keep the pass time under a quarter of wall time", "a deferred drain still runs within k × the last pass duration", the existing follow-up tests adjusted for the deferral; `mix bench get` in full with A1/A2 for `theine`; `mix bench maintenance` for the pass duration. If 25% is not enough for A2 at parallel 4, measure `k` = 7 before touching anything else, and if that fails too, record it under discovered issues rather than relaxing A2. Added 2026-10-28 after M2.8b: the same cap is what the bounded p99 at parallel 1 needs (A1 p99 1.56–2.32× in M2.8b's run). The per-call distribution in §Benchmark log (M2.8b) shows the bounded p99 at 70–140 ns over its p50 when no pass runs and 250–360 ns over it with passes every 64 reads: each trigger that finds the flag clear pays a `send` that wakes the process, and each pass pulls the stripe's cache lines (and the `head` word, which shares a line with `tail`) to the maintenance scheduler. With passes deferred to once per `k × d` the triggers in between are failed compare_exchanges (about 65 ns, 3% of reads at threshold 32) and the cache traffic is once per thousand reads, so the expected p99 is the paused row of that table. If the p99 still misses after the cap, the next lever is the layout: put `head` on its own cache line (`2 + slots` words per stripe becomes `16 + slots`, specs/03 §Layout) so the drainer's head write never invalidates the reader's `tail` line; a bigger `drain_threshold` default is the last resort, since quartering the pass frequency only halved the excess. Met for the duty cycle (specs/04 §The pass, §Decisions M2.8c; specs/08 options table; specs/03 §`schedule_drain/1`): `k` is the `start_link` option `drain_backoff` (default 3, 0 disables) so that benches and tests can set it; every pass records `drain_after = finished + drain_backoff × duration`, a `:drain` arriving earlier is parked with one `Process.send_after/3` timer (`:deferred_drain`, the remaining time rounded up to the millisecond, because BEAM timers are millisecond-grained) with the flag left set, step 2 skips clearing the flag while a deferral is pending, and a second `:drain` in that window is dropped; `run/1`, the interval and `write_batch` passes are never deferred. Tests: maintenance_test "drain-triggered passes under a flooding reader keep the pass time under a quarter of wall time" (300 ms flood; the bound `4 × busy ≤ wall + 3 × the last pass` holds by construction), "a deferred drain runs no earlier than drain_backoff × the last pass duration and within a millisecond of it" (`drain_backoff` 1_000 and the pass handler's emission time), "a :drain during the deferral is dropped, the flag stays set, and run/1 is not deferred" (`drain_backoff` 10_000_000), "drain_backoff: 0 runs every :drain as it arrives"; the `:drain`-after-`run/1` test now waits for its pass; config_test covers the option. Measured with a compiled probe (four pinned readers, Zipf over 1_000 keys, 3 s each, `+sbt db +sbwt very_long`; table under §Benchmark log): the maintenance process goes from 99% busy at `k` 0 to 21% at `k` 3 and 11% at `k` 7, reads/s from 5.77 M to 6.54 M to 6.62 M against 8.06 M unbounded, so `k` 7 buys about 1% and the default stays 3; the remaining 19% gap to the unbounded subject is the hit path (about 60 ns of a 500 ns hit), not maintenance. `mix bench get` in full: 51/60 (36/60 in M2.8b). The bounded p99 at parallel 1 went from 1.56–2.32× to 1.05–1.57×, passing on four of six shapes; A2 at parallel 4 passes on the three binary_1kb shapes (0.71–0.72×) and fails on the three tuple3 shapes (0.57–0.62×), where `theine_unbounded` itself sits at 0.67–0.72× and the bounded subject runs at 0.86–0.90× of it. The residue is discovered issue 6(c) and M2.8d, as this task said to do rather than relax A2. `mix bench maintenance` still reports "no theine subject yet" (M2.11).
- [x] M2.8d **(hot path)** The bounded A1/A2 residue at the hit path (discovered issue 6(c); specs/09 §Decisions): with the duty cycle capped, the bounded hit still misses A2 on the three tuple3 shapes at parallel 4 (0.57–0.62× with `theine_unbounded` at 0.67–0.72×) and A1 p50 at parallel 1 on n=100000 (1.55×), and the bounded overhead over the unbounded hit grows with n at parallel 1 (52 ns at n=1000, 91 ns at n=100000, 99 ns at n=1000000 in M2.8c's run) where a constant two-op cost would not. Measure first, in a compiled loop at each n, which of the three the growth is: the ring's eight cache lines per stripe falling out of L1 behind a larger ETS working set, the `tail`/`head` line bouncing to the maintenance scheduler once per pass, or the id write itself. Levers in order: `head` on its own cache line (`2 + slots` words per stripe becomes `16 + slots`, specs/03 §Layout, so the drainer's head write never invalidates the reader's `tail` line); a smaller ring footprint (32-bit ids are not available in `:atomics`, but `slots` 32 halves the lines); last, record a hit only every second sequence (`band(t, 1)`), which halves the `put`s and the sample at once and would need a specs/03 decision. Gate: `theine` A1/A2 on every shape, or the measured floor recorded in specs/09 §Decisions with the lever that did not reach it. Met by the second clause: the floor is recorded in specs/09 §Decisions M2.8d and the ring is unchanged (specs/03 §Decisions M2.8d). The probe (`bench/results/residue-m28d.exs`, gitignored; tables under §Benchmark log) answered the question with "none of the three": with the maintenance process paused the bounded hit is 44–56 ns over the unbounded one at every n, the two atomics ops and nothing else, and the whole growth with n is the maintenance process running passes concurrently. That growth survives `+S 2` (the two processes on separate physical cores), so it is the pass's memory traffic through the private policy tables and the sketch sharing the 3 MB L3 with the reader, not the SMT sibling, and not the ring: a replica drainer bouncing the stripe once per millisecond costs 1–5 ns. The levers, measured in a replica of `record/2` with that drainer: `head` on its own line 0 ns at one reader and 0–3 at four (the array's alignment is unknown, so the tail still shares a line with the previous stripe's last slots), a slots-first padded layout 0 and 8–16, 32 slots 0, the put on even sequences 0 and 10–25; all inside the bench's wander, none adopted. `drain_backoff` 7 halves the interference at four schedulers (82→72, 102→80, 91→77 ns over unbounded at n = 1000, 100000, 1000000) and would put the n=100000 p50 on the 1.5× line, so the default stays 3 and the pass's footprint is M2.11b. The reference machine is an i5-5257U with two cores and SMT (AGENTS.md): at four readers each atomics op costs about 65 ns, so A2 on the tuple3 shapes (bounded within 3–7% of unbounded) is out of reach of a two-op design there. No `lib/` change, so no new `mix bench get`; M2.8c's table is this tree's.
- [x] M2.9 **(maintenance)** Maintenance restart semantics (specs/04 §Restart semantics): `init/1` resets read-buffer heads to tails, `pending`, the drain flag and the sketch, rebuilds policy and expiry rows from the entries table in `:ets.select/3` chunks of 10_000 via `handle_continue`, all entries into probation, schedules `:drain` if over capacity; `WriteEvents.deliver/2` catches the unregistered-name `ArgumentError` and a call exit and returns `:ok` (specs/03 §Delivery) — tests: maintenance_test "killing Maintenance restarts only Maintenance and every readable entry stays readable", "after a restart with 20_000 entries the policy count equals the table size once idle", "a put during the rebuild is not lost", "writers do not crash when Maintenance is restarting". Met. The rebuild is `Policy.restore/5` (specs/05 §Events `restore`, §Decisions M2.9: probation MRU, no sketch count, a known key skipped by `insert_new`) over an `:ets.select/3` scan with the table held by `safe_fixtable` for its duration, because the ETS docs guarantee a chunked `select` traversal of a `set` only on a fixed table (specs/04 §Decisions M2.9); the continue chain handles no message between chunks, so the events queued during the scan apply after it, as the spec says, and what the chunking buys is the registered name during the scan. `init/1` also calls `ReadBuffer.reset_heads/1`; step 4 goes through `ReadBuffer.schedule_drain/1`. One `[:theine, :maintenance, :rebuild]` event per maintenance start carries `duration`, `entries` and `chunks` (specs/08 §Telemetry, §Decisions M2.9); the 20_000-entry test reads its chunk count from it. The expiry index does not exist yet, so the policy row keeps the stored `expires_at` for M3.1 to index from the same `restore` call (noted there). `pending` after a restart under load can read below zero by at most one per writer per restart (a count taken before the reset, consumed after it); recorded in specs/04 §Decisions M2.9 and asserted as a band. Also tested: a rebuilt cache over `max_size` (150 entries written while paused, then killed) evicting down to 100 through the scheduled `:drain`; the fresh start's rebuild event with 0 entries; `deliver/2` on both paths against an unregistered name and against a stand-in server that dies mid-call (write_events_test); `restore/5` unit cases in policy_test (bounded placement, unbounded row, known key left alone, the settling events, eviction of a rebuilt over-capacity probation). `Theine.Test.Policy` gained `gate/2`/`release/2` (specs/10 §Decisions M2.9) to hold the restarted process inside its first `restore` for the "put during the rebuild" test. `mix bench maintenance` still reports "no theine subject yet" (M2.11); table under §Benchmark log.
- [x] M2.10 **(policy)** `mix bench hitrate` `theine` subject and `window_percent` (specs/09 §`mix bench hitrate`; specs/05 §Regions `window_percent`): the harness drives a real cache calling `run_maintenance/1` every `drain_threshold` accesses; `:rand` seeded deterministically in the maintenance process so results are reproducible; thresholds enforced — outcome: `mix bench hitrate` exits 0 with `theine` ≥ `lru` on every trace and size, and ≥ `lru` + 10 points on `loop` 1_000 and `OLTP` 1_000 and 2_000 (A3, LRU part); table pasted into this file under §Benchmark log. Met for the harness and the thresholds; not met for the OLTP rows, which is open question 1 (discovered issue 7) and keeps `mix bench hitrate` exiting non-zero, as it did between M0.5 and M0.4b. `Theine.Bench.Cache` (`bench/lib`) is the `theine` policy: `Theine.get/2` per access, `Theine.put/3` after a miss, one `Theine.run_maintenance/1` per 32 accesses (`--accesses-per-pass`, 1..64; 1 is Caffeine's synchronous simulator), the cache started with `drain_threshold` 2^30, `maintenance_interval` a day and `stripes: 1` so that the harness's passes are the only ones and a batch's reads drain in access order, and `close/1` raising unless the pass count equals the call count, the cache's tail-sum `hits` equals the replay's, nothing is pending and the cache is within its bound (specs/09 §Decisions M2.10). The first two runs differed by 59–577 hits per OLTP row because the replaying process migrated between schedulers and the stripes drained in a different order; with one stripe two full OLTP runs agree to the hit (333_102, 389_884, 481_168 at 1_000, 2_000, 5_000). `--window-percent 1,5,20` sweeps the window (`theine_w<p>` rows, no threshold; the row at 1 is `theine`). Instances are packed into replay passes within `--memory-gb` (default 4) from measured ETS bytes per entry (theine 378, lru 144 at 1 M entries; DS1's three theine instances would need 5 GB on the 8 GB laptop). theine_test "a key sequence replayed twice through a cache of one name, driven by run_maintenance/1 alone, gives identical hits" pins what the bench relies on. Results (§Benchmark log): `loop` 1_000 58.84% against LRU 0; OLTP +3.61, +0.18 and −1.01 points at 1_000, 2_000 and 5_000, and +2.61, −0.52, −1.24 replayed fully synchronously, with a 20% window adding 4.9, 4.4 and 2.7 points over the 1% one; every other trace is in the full-run table.
- [x] M2.11 **(maintenance)** `mix bench maintenance` `theine` subject: overshoot, pass duration, backpressure fraction (specs/09 §`mix bench maintenance`; specs/04 §Budget): add a `Theine.Bench.Load` subject map to `subjects/0` in `bench/maintenance.exs` (the drivers for the 30 s write run and the mixed run already exist, M0.6). Its `call_writes` callback needs a count of call-path writes from theine. Decide where it lives (a `Maintenance.info/1` field is the cheapest, since the call path is already slow) and record it in specs/03 — outcome: every overshoot sample ≤ `max_size + write_hwm + schedulers_online`, size ≤ `max_size` 1 s after writers stop (A5), pass p99 ≤ 5 ms and max ≤ 20 ms at `max_size` 1_000_000; table pasted under §Benchmark log. Split on 2026-10-29 as M2.8 was: the subject, the count and the measurement are done here; the thresholds fail (1/4) and their fixes are M2.11c and M2.11d below, because each needs spec decisions (discovered issue 8). Done: the `theine` subject is a real cache started with the run's `max_size` and `write_hwm` and the specs/08 defaults, `size` is `:ets.info/2` on the entries table, and `call_writes` is the new `Maintenance.info/1` field of that name, the count of `{:event, _}` calls the maintenance process consumed on either of its paths (`handle_call/3` and the pass's flush), zeroed by `reset/1` (specs/03 §Decisions M2.11); the writer side is untouched. maintenance_test pins the count on both paths (a `pending` held at `write_hwm` makes every put a call; a `run/1` queued ahead of two blocked writers makes the pass's flush consume them) and the reset. Measured (§Benchmark log): the A5 overshoot is 156 k entries over the bound at 1_000_000 and 128 k remain a second after the writers stop, because a pass applies up to 1280 events and evicts at most 1024; the mixed-run pass p99 is 8.5 ms; the backpressure fraction is 0.36%. A prototype of eviction on arrival (M2.11c's design) passes both A5 checks exactly at the bound. `mix bench maintenance` exits non-zero until M2.11c and M2.11d, as `mix bench get` did between M2.8 and M2.8c.
- [x] M2.11c **(maintenance)** **(policy)** Fit the cache as each `:put` is applied, so A5 holds by construction (discovered issue 8; specs/00 A5; specs/03 §Delivery with backpressure "consumed"; specs/04 §The pass; specs/05 `evict()`): in `Maintenance.apply_event/2` a `:put` runs `on_write` then `evict/1` and only then decrements `pending`, so that every entry the table holds beyond `max_size` is counted by `pending` or is an insert in flight; a `:more` from the per-event eviction (an entry heavier than `evict_batch` removals) schedules a `:drain` as the pass does; the pass's `evicted` measurement becomes the count since the previous pass, like `writes` (the pass's own step 5 stays, for rebuilt caches and as the spec's safety net); `Theine.Test.Policy`'s call log and maintenance_test's "writes, reads, evict" order test are adjusted (the per-write evict calls sit inside the writes). Record in specs/03 §Decisions (an event is consumed once the cache fits again), specs/04 §The pass and §Decisions (eviction on arrival; why batched eviction cannot meet the A5 bound as stated), specs/05 §Decisions (`evict()` called per applied `:put` and once per pass). Prototype numbers are in §Benchmark log M2.11 — tests: maintenance_test "under four saturating writers the table never exceeds max_size + pending + writers at any of 200 samples" (the probe's three readings, `max_size` 10_000), "a put that takes the cache over max_size is evicted down before the writer's call returns" (call path, `write_hwm` 1), "the pass's evicted measurement counts the evictions since the previous pass"; `mix bench hitrate` in full (the eviction now precedes the batch's reads; with `--accesses-per-pass 1` nothing changes, so the loop and zipf rows and the OLTP rows at one access per pass must match M2.10's figures); `mix bench maintenance`: both A5 checks pass; tables under §Benchmark log. Met. Built as described, with the decisions in specs/03, 04 and 05 §Decisions M2.11c and the replay-order sentence of specs/09 §Decisions M2.10 updated to match. The call-path test holds `pending` at the default `write_hwm` (as the M2.11 `call_writes` test does) rather than starting with `write_hwm` 1, which made the warm-up puts calls too; the saturating-writers test keeps a writer per scheduler for 200 samples at 5 ms and checks both the `pending`-based bound and the spec's `write_hwm + writers` slack from the table reading alone. The capped-eviction test became "a put heavier than evict_batch removals is capped and follow-up passes evict the rest" (a 50-weight entry into a `window_percent` 80 cache with `evict_batch` 5: five removals on arrival, nine follow-up passes of five, the first pass reporting `evicted` 10). The M2.9 restart test's key 1 now ends in protected rather than the window, since the eviction after each later put overflowed it into probation before the pass's reads promoted it. `mix bench maintenance` (§Benchmark log): both A5 checks pass, the worst sample exactly at the bound (1_001_028) and the table at 1_000_000 a second after the writers stop; the write run's throughput rose 3% (2_796_211 against 2_717_273 puts in 30 s); the pass budget still fails (p99 9.4 ms, max 23.5 ms), which is M2.11d.
- [x] M2.11d **(maintenance)** The pass budget under a saturated mailbox (discovered issue 8; specs/04 §The pass step 1, §Budget): with events applied on arrival the step-1 flush only keeps step 3 from reading a stale policy, yet under load it is the whole pass (about 970 flushed events at about 7 µs each with their evictions in the mixed run, p99 8.9 ms, max 21.3 ms at 1_000_000 with the M2.11c prototype). Measure first with the pass telemetry split by step (flush, drain, evict, age) at 1_000_000 in the mixed run; then cap the flush at `write_batch` (256) rather than `write_batch * 4`, or make the cap a `start_link` option, with a specs/04 §Decisions entry (the spec says raise caps rather than remove them; this lowers one, so say why: the flush does no work the mailbox would not do anyway, and the budget is the pass's latency, not its throughput). Gate: `mix bench maintenance` pass p99 ≤ 5 ms and max ≤ 20 ms at 1_000_000 with both A5 checks still passing, or the measured floor recorded in specs/04 §Decisions with the step breakdown; the write run's `writes` (throughput under backpressure) must not drop by more than 10%. Met. The flush cap is `write_batch` (a constant, no new option), with the why in specs/04 §Decisions M2.11d and §The pass step 1, §Budget and §Acceptance updated. The measurement used a temporary, uncommitted per-step instrumentation of `pass/1` and the probe `bench/results/pass-steps-m211d.exs` (gitignored; its header describes the instrumentation). In the mixed run at 1_000_000 the pass was the flush: 1024 events at 6.3 µs each, 6.3 ms of a 6.5 ms p50 and all of the slowest 1%. Drain took 0.5 ms and evict and age about 1 µs. With the cap at 256 the pass p99 was 3.2 ms in the probe with unchanged mixed-run throughput (tables under §Benchmark log). maintenance_test's caps test now pins `write_batch` events per pass. `mix bench maintenance`: 4/4 thresholds in both runs of this tree (pass p99 3.3 ms, max 5.9 and 6.1 ms, A5 at the bound). The write run's `writes` was 2_477_144 and 2_710_076 against M2.11c's 2_796_211, and 2_340_764 with the old cap on the same day. That figure moves ±15% between runs whatever the cap, and the new cap is not below the old one's same-day run. No `mix bench hitrate`: its harness runs a pass every 32 accesses, so the flush never sees more than 32 events under either cap. Not covered: sketch aging. A 16_384-word chunk took 2.4 ms under load at 10_000, so a pass that ages and flushes a full cap can reach about 6 ms. The 30 s bench at 1_000_000 never starts a reset, and at that size aging falls in under 1% of passes. Repeating the suite turned up three flakes (discovered issue 9). Two were test bugs, fixed here: the pass handler in maintenance_test now matches its own cache, and policy_test waits for its named table to be freed. The third is M2.11e.
- [x] M2.11e **(policy)** Make replays deterministic again under eviction on arrival (discovered issue 9; specs/09 §Decisions M2.10): theine_test "a key sequence replayed twice through a cache of one name, driven by run_maintenance/1 alone, gives identical hits" fails about once in 120 full-suite runs (`mix test --repeat-until-failure 150`; 300 runs of the test alone all pass), with both replays at 626 passes, all requested. Confirm the suspected cause first. Since M2.11c a `:put` evicts when the maintenance process receives it, not in the next requested pass, so whether a miss's eviction lands before or after the replaying process's next `get`s depends on scheduling. `Theine.Bench.Cache` has the same get-then-put-on-miss shape, so the claim that two `mix bench hitrate` runs agree to the hit is at risk too. Check it by running `--traces OLTP` twice. Likely fix, in the drivers only (no `lib/` change): make each miss's put synchronous with its eviction, e.g. a `Theine.Maintenance.info/1` round trip after the put (`write_hwm` must be ≥ 1, so the call path cannot be forced for every put). Cost it on the full hit-rate run. Record the change in specs/09 §Decisions M2.10. Tests: the replay test passes 300 full-suite repeats; two `mix bench hitrate --traces OLTP` runs agree to the hit; `mix bench hitrate` in full, with the rows compared to M2.11c's. Met, with the fix as proposed (specs/09 §Decisions M2.11e). The cause is confirmed with `bench/results/replay-m211e.exs` (gitignored) on the test's key sequence. Every eviction landing before the next get gives 15_298 hits; every eviction landing after the batch's gets (maintenance paused) gives 15_279. Sixty unsynchronised replays, quiet and under a busy spinner per scheduler, all gave 15_279, because the replayer rarely yields within a 32-access batch; when it does, an eviction lands earlier and the count drifts. `Theine.Bench.Cache.access/2` and the test now follow a miss's put with a `Theine.Maintenance.info/1` round trip, which takes the "before" order, Caffeine's simulator order. No `lib/` change. The replay test passed 301 full-suite runs (`mix test --repeat-until-failure 300`, 262 tests each). Two `--traces OLTP` runs, both made while that loop ran, agree to the hit (theine 324_531, 382_947, 479_056). M2.11c's full hit-rate run was never recorded (its commit was closed by hand), so the full run here is compared with M2.10's. OLTP moves to 35.50, 41.89 and 52.40, within 0.06 points of M2.10's one-access-per-pass figures. `loop` rises 0.45 to 59.29. Every other row is within 0.13 points. The bench exits non-zero on OLTP 2000 (−0.58) and 5000 (−1.25) `theine ≥ lru`, which is M2.13's. The cost: the full run took 84.5 min against M2.10's 46.3. A `bench/results/sync-cost-m211e.exs` probe puts the round trip at about 5.7 µs per miss on top of about 7.9 µs for the put and its eviction. `policy_row/2` saves only 1.3 µs of that, so `info/1` stays. Table under §Benchmark log M2.11e.
- [x] M2.11b **(maintenance)** The pass's memory footprint per `on_access` (discovered issue 6(d); specs/04 §Budget): M2.8d measured that a running maintenance process costs a single reader 20–50 ns per hit at n ≥ 100000 even on a separate physical core (specs/09 §Decisions M2.8d), with 1.7 µs per `on_access` through `ids`, `policy`, `order` and the sketch. Measure first, with the M2.8d probe's "running minus paused" rows and the pass telemetry: how many cache lines a pass touches per drained read (ETS bucket arrays and objects of the three private tables, four sketch lines) and which table dominates. Levers in order: fold `ids` into the `policy` table or key the order table so that `on_access` does two lookups and one move instead of three lookups; skip the sketch increment for an id that is already in protected (Caffeine counts every access, so this needs a specs/02 or 05 decision and a `mix bench hitrate` check); a `drain_backoff` of 7 only if the footprint cannot be cut (it halves the policy's sample under saturation). Gate: the running-minus-paused gap at one reader under 20 ns at n = 100000 and 1000000 in the probe, `mix bench hitrate` unchanged, `mix bench maintenance` pass p99 unchanged or better; or the floor recorded in specs/04 §Decisions. Met by the second clause: the floor is in specs/04 §Decisions M2.11b, with the gap at 37–38 ns. The measurement overturned the premise. In the probe the unpinned maintenance process ran on the pinned reader's own scheduler for every pass at `+S 4`, and for 73–100% of them at `+S 2`, so M2.8d's "other core" row was not one (specs/09 §Decisions M2.11b). The gap is the pass's CPU time on the reader's scheduler, not L3 traffic: a replica pass pinned to the other scheduler cost the reader −6 to +16 ns. The gap therefore scales with the cost per drained read, and that was cut by about a quarter with no change to any policy decision. Three changes: integer `order` keys (specs/05 §Decisions M2.11b; the move went from about 950 to about 520 ns at n = 100_000), the sketch hash in 16-bit halves (specs/02 §Decisions M2.11b; about 95 ns of bignum arithmetic gone), and one struct update per hit instead of two. Measured A/B in one VM, with the previous revision's modules run through the `policy_module` hook: `on_access` 2412→1755 ns and 2869→2070 ns, pass duty 8.4→6.1% and 9.3→7.0%, median gap 42→38 ns and 54→37 ns at n = 100_000 and 1_000_000. `mix bench maintenance` pass p99 3336→2815–2900 µs. `mix bench hitrate` gives the same hits on every row (§Benchmark log M2.11b). Not taken, with reasons in specs/04: folding `ids` (one lookup, about 8%), skipping the sketch on protected hits (a policy change), and `drain_backoff` 7 (by the timer arithmetic, no change at this pass length; not measured).
- [x] M2.12 Stress tier for M2 (specs/03 §Acceptance write events and backpressure; specs/09 §Bounded; specs/04 §Acceptance restart; specs/10 §Acceptance): `@tag :stress` tests — tests: stress/write_events_test "1_000_000 puts across all schedulers end with policy count equal to table size", "backpressure keeps the mailbox under write_hwm + schedulers through a 10 s paused window", stress/bounded_test "entry count never exceeds max_size + write_hwm + schedulers under a 100% write workload", stress/restart_test "killing Maintenance under load with 100_000 entries loses no readable entry and is back under max_size within two intervals"; `mix test --include stress` completes under 10 minutes. Met. The four tests live in `test/stress/` (`write_events_test.exs`, `backpressure_test.exs`, `bounded_test.exs`, `restart_test.exs`), `async: false` with writers pinned per scheduler. The write-event test runs twice, unbounded and at `max_size` 50_000, over a shared 100_000-key space with a delete after every eighth put, so writers race on keys (discovered issue 2). Each run checks every table key's policy row id as well as the counts. The backpressure test asserts at 10 ms samples through the 10 s paused window that exactly `write_hwm` puts returned and that the mailbox and `pending` stay at most `write_hwm + schedulers`. The bounded test runs for 10 s at `max_size` 100_000, and once idle `evictions == puts − size` holds exactly. The restart test kills an unpaused maintenance process a quarter of the way into 40_000 inserts against a full 100_000 cache, with readers running. The policy is back within `max_size` well inside two intervals. Every snapshot entry that is missing is covered by evictions, and `puts − (size + evictions)` is 0 or 1: a `:kill` between an eviction's `delete_object` and its counter add loses that one count. That test found a bug: once idle the policy held 19–23 rows with no entry, so it was `max_size` while the table was 23 short. A key the scan restored was evicted before its queued `:put` arrived, and that `:put` re-created the row. Fixed with a `restored_id` mark in `Theine.Policy`: an unknown-key `:put` at or below the mark is applied only if the table still holds that id (specs/05 §Decisions M2.12, specs/04 §Restart semantics). Deterministic tests: policy_test "a queued put for a restored entry evicted since does not bring its row back", "the restored-id mark only rises"; maintenance_test "a restored entry evicted before its queued put arrives leaves no row" (through the restore gate). `mix bench hitrate` was not run: replays never restart the maintenance process, so the mark stays 0 and every decision is unchanged by construction. The mixed run's pass gains one integer compare per new-key `:put`. `mix test --include stress`: 273 passed in 41 s.
- [x] M2.13 **(policy)** **(maintenance)** Adaptive window, on by default (specs/05 §Adaptive window; specs/04 §The pass step 7; specs/08 `adaptive` default true; specs/09 §`mix bench hitrate` thresholds; specs/00 A3; human decision 2026-10-29 resolving open question 1, replaces M6.1): hill climber every `10 * max_size` sampled requests, 6.25% initial step decaying by 0.98, restart on a 5-point delta, resizing at the LRU ends; the sample counts drained read-buffer accesses as hits and `:put` events for keys new to the policy as misses, so it works with `stats: false` and adds nothing to the hit path; `adaptive` defaults to true (config_test updated) and `adaptive: false` reproduces every existing policy test; check whether specs/05's immediate demotion still matches Caffeine's deferred demotion once the window moves and record it in specs/05 §Decisions; `mix bench hitrate` gains a `theine_static` subject (`adaptive: false`) and the check `theine ≥ theine_static − 1` on every row — tests: policy_test "adaptive: false leaves every existing policy test result unchanged", "the climber grows the window on a recency trace and shrinks it on a frequency trace", "window and probation weights stay consistent across a resize" (property), "the climber samples with stats: false"; bench: full `mix bench hitrate` exits 0: `theine` ≥ `lru` on every row including OLTP, ≥ `lru` + 10 on `loop` 1_000, ≥ `theine_static` − 1 on every row (A3); `mix bench maintenance` pass p99 no worse than before the task; both tables under §Benchmark log. Met. `Policy.climb/1` runs in pass step 7 and follows Caffeine's `determineAdjustment`/`increaseWindow`/`decreaseWindow` (specs/05 §Adaptive window, now spelled out, and §Decisions M2.13): the step starts negative as Caffeine's does, growing takes probation then protected LRU entries and moves the limit out of `protected_max`, at most 1000 entries move per call and the rest waits in `adjustment`, and `clear/1` restarts the climber. The sample counts sit in the struct (`sample_hits` folded into the drain's one struct update, `sample_misses` in `insert`), and the cold fields in a `climber` map that is `nil` with `adaptive: false`. Immediate demotion still matches Caffeine's deferred one; the argument is in specs/05 §Decisions M2.13. `Policy.resize/2` and `Policy.climber/1` are public for tests, and `Maintenance.info/1` gained `climber`. `Theine.Test.Policy` forwards `climb/1`, so the call-order test now ends in `:climb`. Tests: the four named ones plus unit tests for the first steps, grow, the demotion on grow, shrink, the 1000-entry cap, `adaptive: false` and `reset/1`. The policy-level replays use a synthetic recency trace (window 2 → 153 at `max_size` 200, +18.8 points) and Zipf(0.9) (window 80 → 12). `mix bench hitrate`: every row passes, OLTP at +6.94/+2.97/+1.51 over LRU, and `theine_static` reproduces M2.11e's hits exactly. `mix bench maintenance` p99 2903.93/2851.95 against 2814.61/2899.83 the same day. The full hitrate run now takes two invocations (AGENTS.md).

## M3 Expiration

Done: M3.1, M3.2.

- [x] M3.1 **(maintenance)** `Theine.Expiry` index and sweep in the pass (specs/06 §Index, §Semantics bullet 4; specs/04 §The pass step 4): `expiry` ordered_set `{{expires_at, key}, id}` maintained from write events using the previous `expires_at` kept on the policy row, `sweep/2` capped by `expire_batch` with removal by id compare, `expirations` counter, follow-up `:drain` when the cap is hit; the restart rebuild (M2.9) must index every entry it restores with a finite `expires_at`, from the same `Policy.restore/5` call or beside it in `Maintenance.rebuild/3`, and `Policy.reset/1` must empty the index for `clear/1` — tests: expiry_test "with maintenance running and no reads an expired entry leaves the table within one maintenance_interval", "100_000 entries expiring at once are removed over several passes of at most expire_batch each without waiting for the timer", "a put racing the sweep of the previous version keeps the new value", "an unbounded cache sweeps expired entries too"; `mix bench maintenance` mixed run stays under `pass_budget`.
- [x] M3.2 `expire/3`, `persist/2`, `ttl/2` (specs/06 §Semantics bullets 5–7; specs/08 §Calls): `expire/3` updates element 6 in place and sends `{:put, key, id, weight, expires_at}` with the unchanged id; the policy treats a same-id `:put` as an expiry/weight update with no sketch increment and no MRU move (specs/05 §`on_write`) — tests: expiry_test "expire/3 moves the entry in the index and it is swept at the new time with the old row gone", "persist/2 removes the index row and the entry never expires", "ttl/2 returns remaining ms, :infinity, or :error", "expire/3 on an absent key returns false". Met. `Theine.expire/3`, `persist/2` and `ttl/2` delegate to `Entry.expire/3` and `Entry.ttl/2`. An expired key counts as absent and is left in place. The event names the version the table holds after the update, since a racing put may have replaced the one looked up. The rewrite keeps the id, which breaks every remover's "a failed `delete_object` means another version" assumption, so four checks against the entries table went in (specs/06 and specs/05 §Decisions M3.2). `delete/2` starts again when its `delete_object` missed a same-id rewrite; that is one more `lookup_element` per delete, and the tracing test's delete multiset now counts it. `on_remove` ignores a current-id removal while the table holds that id. A same-id `:put` takes `expires_at` from the table. A known-key `:put` with an id below the row's is applied only while the table holds that id. The new put/expire race test found the last one: its row ended on another version than the table once in 30 repeats. Eviction or sweep losing its delete to a rewrite miscounts by one and the `expire/3` event re-admits the key; that is accepted. Tests: the four named ones plus expiry_test "expire/3 sets an expiry on an entry that had none and can shorten one", "expire/3 rejects an expiry that is not a positive integer or :infinity", "expire/3 is not an access: the policy row keeps its place", "an expired read that loses its delete to expire/3 leaves the entry and its row", and two real-interleaving loops (delete vs expire: the key is always gone; put, two expires and a persist: the policy row and index match the table's version); policy_test "a same-id write takes expires_at from the entries table while it holds that id", "an older id's late :put is applied only while the entries table holds that id", "on_remove with the current id is ignored while the entries table still holds that id". Two existing tests sent a removal for a tuple still in the table, or a same-id event the table contradicted; they now delete or rewrite the table first, as the real callers do. `mix bench maintenance` and `mix bench get` under §Benchmark log M3.2.

## M4 Loading

Done: M4.1, M4.2, M4.3, M4.4, M4.4b, M4.5. M4 complete.

- [x] M4.1 `Theine.Loader` single-flight `fetch/3` and `fetch/2` (specs/07 §The loader function, §Single-flight, §Why not a GenServer per key; specs/08 §Calls `fetch`): `inflight` and `waiters` tables in Owner, claim via `insert_new`, waiter registration with monitor and double-check, release-before-notify in `after`, retry up to 3 attempts then uncoalesced load, `{:commit, v}`, `{:commit, v, ttl: ms}`, `{:ignore, v}`, raise/throw/exit propagated to the claimant and delivered as `{:error, {:load_failed, reason}}` to waiters with the four reason shapes in specs/07 §The loader function, `loads` and `load_failures` counters, `[:theine, :load, :start | :stop | :exception]` telemetry, `fetch/2` without any loader raises `ArgumentError` — tests: loader_test "1_000 concurrent fetches on one missing key run the loader once and all receive the value", "{:ignore, v} is returned to every waiter and the key stays absent", "a raising loader re-raises in the claimant, every waiter gets {:error, {:load_failed, _}}, nothing is cached, inflight and waiters are empty", "per-call loader wins over the cache loader", "fetch/2 without a loader raises ArgumentError". Met. `Theine.fetch/2,4` delegate to `Theine.Loader.fetch/4`. `Theine.Owner` creates the two tables, named by the new `%Config{}` fields `inflight` and `waiters`; a compiled probe saw no change in the `get/2` hit from the two extra keys (specs/07 §Decisions M4.1). Fetchers that must overlap are gated: the claimant blocks until the test has seen every other fetcher's waiter row, so the coalescing tests are deterministic rather than timed. The step-4 receive is complete here rather than in M4.2, since a waiter without `:DOWN` or `after` handling would hang on a dead claimant. Writing it showed that the spec as written left a killed claimant's claim in `inflight` forever: the next fetcher monitored the dead pid, got `:noproc` and failed. Now any `:DOWN` deletes the dead claim by exact object, and `:noproc` retries like `:normal`. Waiters monitor with `alias: :demonitor` and the claimant sends to the alias, so a timed-out waiter gets no late message (specs/07 §Decisions M4.1, with the reason shapes, invalid results, options and counting rules the spec left open). Tests: the five named ones plus loader_test "a throw or an exit reaches the claimant as such and the waiters as {:nocatch, v} or the exit reason" (and an Erlang `:badarg` normalized for waiters), "a loader returning anything else raises ArgumentError in the claimant and fails the waiters", "a hit returns the cached value and never calls the loader", "{:commit, v, ttl: ms} stores the value with that TTL", "invalid loaders and options raise ArgumentError", "the claimant's exception keeps its stacktrace", "a load emits start and stop, a failed one start and exception, keyed by cache only"; config_test names the two tables; theine_test checks they are removed on stop and recreated by an Owner restart, with a `fetch` working afterwards.
- [x] M4.2 Waiter edge cases: claimant death, timeout, late registration (specs/07 §Single-flight step 4 and step 6): `{:DOWN, …}` handling with the cached-value check and the `:normal` retry, `timeout:` option default 5000. The code landed in M4.1 (including the dead-claim deletion, the `:noproc` retry and the alias monitor of specs/07 §Decisions M4.1); this task proves it and fixes what the tests find. Also assert that a timed-out waiter's mailbox holds no `{:theine_loaded, _, _}` after the claimant finishes — tests: loader_test "claimant killed mid-load gives waiters {:error, {:load_failed, :killed}} and the next fetch claims successfully", "a waiter with timeout: 10 and a 100 ms loader returns {:error, :timeout} and leaves no waiter row", "a waiter registering after the claimant's release retries and hits the committed value", "a claimant that exits :normal after committing leaves waiters with the value". Met. The interleavings are fixed rather than raced: `Theine.Loader` calls a `pause/2` macro between steps, which the test build compiles to `Theine.Test.LoaderHook.pause/2` (`config :theine, loader_hook:`) and every other build to `:ok`, as `Theine.Clock` does. A fetcher arms one point (`:claim`, `:claim_lost`, `:monitored`, `:registered`, `:timed_out`) and blocks there until the test resumes it (specs/07 §Decisions M4.2, specs/10 §Required helpers). The seam found discovered issue 12, an exactly-once hole in step 1, fixed here: the claimant looks the key up again with the new `Theine.Entry.peek/2` before loading. Tests: the four named ones; the `:normal` claimant is played by hand, since the library's claimant always notifies. Also loader_test "a claim left by a claimant killed with no waiters is reclaimed by the next fetch" (`:noproc`), "a claimant killed after committing leaves waiters with the committed value", "a claimant that exits :normal without committing sends its waiters to load once" (20 waiters, one load), "a notification sent to a timed-out waiter's alias is dropped" (the test sends to the alias it read from the waiter row after the timeout, then checks the waiter's mailbox is empty), "a waiter whose timeout fires as the claimant notifies returns the value" (`:timed_out`), "a waiter that registered before the release but double-checks after it takes the notification" (`:registered`; stats show no second `get`), "a fetcher whose claim lost to a claimant that then finished retries and hits" (`:claim_lost`), "a fetcher that missed before the commit and claims after the release does not load again" (`:claim`; this test fails without the fix). The 5000 ms default is not timed in a test. 200 repeats of loader_test and 10 of the full suite passed. `mix bench get` is a regression check only (`get/2` and `put/4` are untouched); table under §Benchmark log M4.2.
