- [x] M4.3 `fetch_async/4` (specs/07 §Asynchronous; specs/08 §Calls): `Task.Supervisor.async_nolink` under `{Theine.TaskSupervisor, name}`, `Task.completed/1` on a hit — tests: loader_test "ten fetch_async tasks for one key run the loader once", "a loader crash surfaces through Task.yield as {:exit, _} and does not crash the caller", "fetch_async on a hit returns an already-completed task". Met. `Theine.fetch_async/2,3,4` delegate to `Theine.Loader.fetch_async/4`. The caller resolves the loader, validates the options and does the lookup, so `ArgumentError` raises in the caller and a hit spawns nothing. A miss's task enters the single-flight at the claim, so an async miss counts once in `stats/1` (specs/07 §Decisions M4.3). Further tests cover tasks and `fetch/4` callers sharing one load, throw and exit reaching `Task.yield`/`Task.await`, `{:ignore, v}` and `ttl:`, the cache loader, a waiter task's `timeout:`, and caller-side `ArgumentError`. Not a hot path: `get/2` is unchanged.
- [x] M4.4 **(hot path)** Refresh-after-write and `refresh/2` (specs/07 §Refresh-after-write; specs/08 §Calls `refresh`, §Telemetry): `refreshing` table in Owner, check on hit only when `refresh_after_write` is set, background task under the Task.Supervisor, old value kept on `:ignore` or error with a `:warning` log omitting the key and `[:theine, :refresh, :exception]` telemetry, `refreshes` counter, `refresh/2` returning `:ok | {:error, :refreshing | :no_loader}` — tests: refresh_test "with refresh_after_write: 20 a value written at t=0 is served unchanged at t=25 without waiting", "100 concurrent readers at t=25 trigger exactly one refresh and the new value is served after it", "a refresh loader that raises leaves the old value and the key refreshable again", "refresh/2 returns {:error, :refreshing} while one runs and {:error, :no_loader} without a loader", "a refresh for a key deleted during the load still stores the value"; tracing_test "hit on a cache without refresh_after_write is unchanged"; `mix bench get` unchanged. Met. `Theine.Owner` wraps the config's `hit` field as `{:refresh, ms, hit}` only when both `refresh_after_write` and a cache `loader:` are set, and `get/2` matches that 3-tuple as one more clause of its existing case, so every other cache runs the same hit (specs/07 §Decisions M4.4). `fetch/4` and `fetch_async/4` in a refreshing cache look up through `Theine.refreshing_get/3`, so a stale hit refreshes with the per-call loader. `Theine.refresh/2` delegates to `Theine.Loader.refresh/2`; the task is `Theine.Loader`'s, a load like a claimant's (telemetry, `loads`, `load_failures`), deleting its `refreshing` row by exact object in `after`. `refreshes` counts refreshes started. The failure log carries the exception banner only (a stacktrace's top frame can hold the key). Further tests: no refresh before the age, a refreshed value fresh for another window, throw/exit/`{:ignore, v}`/`ttl:`, TTL expiry beats refresh, a bounded cache, a cache with `refresh_after_write` and no loader (`get/2` does not refresh, `fetch/4` does), `fetch_async` on a stale hit, `refresh/2` on an absent key, a refresh whose task cannot start gives its claim back, and the two telemetry shapes; tracing_test asserts the unchanged hit for a cache with a loader but no refresh, with refresh but no loader, and for a fresh hit, and a stale hit's one `insert_new` plus the `refreshes` op.
- [x] M4.4b A refresh task killed from outside must not block its key (specs/07 §Decisions M4.4, open entry; discovered issue 13): a task killed by `Process.exit(pid, :kill)`, or by a task-supervisor restart while Owner lives, leaves its `refreshing` row, and the key never refreshes again. Recommended: the task writes its pid into its row first thing (`:ets.update_element/3`; only the task deletes the row, so nothing races it), and a stale hit or `refresh/2` whose `insert_new` fails looks the row up and, when it holds a dead pid, deletes it by exact object and claims again. Cost: one lookup and one `Process.alive?/1` per stale hit while a refresh runs, in refreshing caches only — tests: refresh_test "a refresh task killed mid-load leaves the key refreshable", "a task supervisor restart mid-refresh leaves the key refreshable"; specs/07 §Decisions entry closed. Met, with one addition to the recommendation. The row is `{key, ref, pid | nil}` (a fresh ref replaces the unread `started_at`). The task's own write alone leaves `nil` forever when the task is killed before its first instruction, so the starter also writes the pid once `start_child` returns. It writes with `:ets.select_replace/2` on `{key, ref, nil}`, a compare-and-swap: an `update_element/3` there could mark a newer claim's row with this starter's finished task, and the next claim would then start a second refresh beside a live one. The replacement's key comes from `{:element, 1, :"$_"}`, because `select_replace` rejects a `:const` key when the key holds a map or a match-spec atom (found by the test below; specs/07 §Decisions M4.4b). Two `Theine.Test.LoaderHook` points make the windows deterministic: `:refresh_started` (starter, before its write) and `:refresh_task` (task, before its own write, armed in the starter and found through `$callers`, cleared with the new `disarm/1`). Further tests: "a task killed before it writes its pid was named by its starter", "a task whose starter died before writing its pid writes it itself", "a starter's late write leaves a newer refresh's row alone" (fails with `update_element/3` in place of the compare-and-swap), "a key that is not a valid match pattern still gets its task's pid"; tracing_test now counts the winning stale hit's `select_replace`, and a new test pins the losing stale hit (two lookups, one `insert_new`, one counter op). Not a hot path: `get/2`, `put/4`, `ReadBuffer` and `Entry` are unchanged, and only a stale hit in a refreshing cache reaches the new code, so `mix bench get` was not run.
- [x] M4.5 Stress tier for M4 and leak checks (specs/07 §Acceptance exactly-once soak, no leaks; specs/10 §Acceptance): — tests: stress/loader_test "exactly-once holds across 100 rounds of 1_000 fetchers on random keys with random claimant delays", "after the soak inflight, waiters and refreshing are empty and the task supervisor has no children"; `mix test --include stress` still completes under 10 minutes. Met; `lib/` unchanged. Each exactly-once round takes 1–20 fresh keys, a 0–50 ms loader delay per key and a 0–50 ms start offset per fetcher, so a round has claimants, waiters, late registrations and hits; every fetcher must get its key's value, every key fetched must load exactly once and no other key at all, and `loads` must equal the keys loaded. The leak test runs its own soak, since the exactly-once one never reaches `refreshing` or the task supervisor: 100 rounds of 1_000 random operations (`fetch/4` and `fetch_async/4` with commit, `ttl:`, ignore, raise, throw and exit loaders and half of them with a 1–20 ms `timeout:`, `get/2`, `refresh/2`, `put/4`, `delete/2`) on a 64-key pool in a cache with `refresh_after_write: 20` and a cache loader whose behaviour the key carries, the injected clock advanced 0–35 ms per round. It asserts every outcome is a shape specs/07 names with the operation's own key, that the soak reached waiter timeouts, failed loads in claimants, waiters and async tasks, and refreshes from both `refresh/2` and stale hits, then waits for the task supervisor to empty and checks all three tables. All randomness is drawn in the test process, so the ExUnit seed reproduces a round. The soak kills nothing: a killed claimant's or refresh task's row stays until the next claim of its key by design (M4.1, M4.4b). Mutants: dropping the claimant's `waiters` cleanup fails the leak test; reverting M4.2's `peek` (discovered issue 12) passes both soaks, as issue 12 predicted, and the `:claim` test in loader_test remains its guard. The module takes about 10 s; `mix test --include stress` 58 s (388 tests), and the module passed 9 seeds in a row.

- [x] M5.6b The A1 p99 at n=1000 parallel 1 inside the development band (discovered issue 4; open question 4): find out what puts the bounded `theine` p99 at 1.67–2.02× `ets` on this row while `ets` p99 holds at 363–367 ns. A compiled probe at n=1000 with one pinned reader should record each read's latency and, from a pass telemetry handler, each pass's start, end and `scheduler_id`. Report how many reads above the `theine` p99 overlap a pass on the reader's scheduler, and how much `drain_backoff` and `read_buffer` sizing move the row. Change `lib/` only for a lever that stays inside specs/03 and specs/04, and record it in their §Decisions. Never touch the band or the threshold — outcome: either a full `mix bench get --dev-floor` exits 0, or its `--sizes 1000` rerun does (specs/09 §Decisions M1.6), and the run is recorded under §Final gate. Or, if no lever exists, the measurement is recorded under §Benchmark log, and open question 4 states what it showed for the human. M6 stays blocked until this is checked or open question 4 is answered. Met by the second outcome: no lever exists, and `lib/` is unchanged (§Benchmark log M5.6b). Two compiled probes did the measuring. One is Benchee as the bench calls it, run for ten interleaved rounds per variant. The other times every read of a pinned reader against each pass's start, end and scheduler. With maintenance paused for the whole run, the bounded p99/ets was past 1.65 in 5 of 10 Benchee rounds (median 1.70). The default cache was past it in 4 of 10 (median 1.65). Every pass ran on the pinned reader's own scheduler. The reads that overlap a pass are 0.14% of all reads and about 2% of the reads above p99. Adding the 20 µs after each pass brings that to 4–5%, and leaving those reads out lowers the p99 by 2–3 ns. Within the rounds' spread, neither `drain_backoff` 7 or 15 nor `read_buffer_slots` 16, 256 or 1024 nor `drain_threshold` 128 moved the row past paused. `drain_backoff: 0` made it worse (2.14). So the tail is in the bounded hit, not in the passes, and specs/03 and 04 have no lever for it. Open question 4 carries the measurement for the human. Two side findings are in AGENTS.md: Benchee caps a row at 1_000_000 samples, about 0.5 s at this shape, and a per-read probe needs a large reader heap.

## M6 Optional (only after M5.6b is checked)

Done: M6.2, M6.3, M6.4. M6 complete.

- M6.1 (adaptive window) moved to M2.13 on 2026-10-29; it is now the default policy.
- [x] M6.2 Opt-in eviction listener (specs/00 §Non-goals; specs/08 §Telemetry `[:theine, :eviction]`): `[:theine, :eviction]` telemetry per removal only when `eviction_listener: true` — tests: maintenance_test "no eviction event is emitted by default", "with the listener enabled one event per eviction and expiration carries the cause"; `mix bench maintenance` pass p99 still under `pass_budget` with the listener off. Met. `eviction_listener` is a boolean `start_link` option (default false; specs/08 options table, `start_link/1` doc, config_test). The event has `%{weight: w}` as measurements and `%{cache, key, value, cause}` as metadata. `cause` is `:size` or `:expired`, the removals Caffeine's `evictionListener` sees. `delete/2`, `clear/1` and overwrites emit nothing, so every event has exactly one `evictions` or `expirations` count (specs/08 §Decisions M6.2). It fires at the four places those counters are added. Two are in the maintenance process, through `Policy.remove/2` (`:size`) and `Policy.expire_key/5` (the sweep). The other two are in the caller, through `Entry.expired/2` (the reader's expired read) and `put_new`'s expired branch, so an expiry that a read finds first is not lost. Neither is the hit path. With the option off, a removal costs one field match on the policy struct (its new `listener` field: the cache name, or nil) or one config field read. No call is added, so the tracing multisets are unchanged. Unlike the load events, the metadata carries the key and value, since naming the entry is what a listener is for. The handler runs in the process that removed the entry, and the `start_link/1` doc tells users to keep it fast. The two tests share one scenario at `max_size` 100. Keys 1..60 are written with a TTL and the clock passes it. Key 1 is read and key 2 is `put_new`'d, so the caller deletes each. A pass sweeps 3..60, and 150 more keys evict 51. With the listener on there are 60 `:expired` and 51 `:size` events, equal to `stats/1`. Each carries its entry's key, value and weight. Keys 1 and 2 are emitted by the test process, 3..60 and every size eviction by the maintenance process. The evicted keys plus the table's keys are exactly the keys written. The default cache runs the same scenario and gets no events. `mix bench maintenance` (listener off) passes 4/4 with p99 4.03 ms, against the final gate's 3.96. `Theine.Entry` is a hot-path module, so `mix bench get` ran too, though `get/2`'s hit is unchanged (§Benchmark log M6.2). The config struct had 36 keys before the new field and was already a hashed map, so the hit's one-key lookup did not change shape.
- [x] M6.3 `fetch_all/3` (specs/00 §Non-goals): bulk fetch coalescing per key through the existing single-flight — tests: loader_test "fetch_all returns a map of every key with hits served from the cache and misses loaded once each", "a loader failure for one key does not lose the other keys' results". Met. `Theine.fetch_all(cache, keys, loader \\ nil, opts \\ [])` returns `%{key => {:ok, v} | {:error, reason}}`. It goes through each distinct key of any enumerable in order, in the caller, with `fetch/4`'s lookup and claim-or-wait. That is Caffeine's `getAll` for a loader without `loadAll`, which loads sequentially. Duplicates are looked up and counted once. When this process's claimed load fails, the key gets `{:error, {:load_failed, reason}}`, the reply its waiters got, instead of a raise. `timeout:` is per key wait. A missing loader or a bad option raises before any lookup. There is no bulk loader. Claiming every key first would make waits while holding claims possible, which the per-key protocol never does. specs/07 has a new §Bulk section, an acceptance line and a §Decisions M6.3 entry. specs/08 §Calls, the specs/00 non-goal and Caffeine mapping, and the README (calls, mapping, "Not in v1") follow. In the first test, another fetcher holds key 3's claim, so `fetch_all` waits on it and loads only key 4, and duplicates count once in `stats/1`. The second test covers raise, throw, exit, `:ignore` and `:commit` in one call, plus a waiter on `fetch_all`'s own failed claim; afterwards the failed keys load again. Two more tests cover a per-key timeout, the cache loader through `fetch_all/2`, and the `ArgumentError`s. There is a doctest with a hit, a duplicate and a failing key. The gate passes: 410 tests, Loader and Policy at 100.00%, dialyzer clean, `mix docs` silent. No hot-path module changed, so no bench ran.
- [x] M6.4 Expire-after-access design note (specs/00 §Non-goals): `specs/11-expire-after-access.md` draft describing how the read buffer would carry access time to the policy and what it costs the hot-path budget — outcome: the note exists, cross-references specs/01, 03 and 06, and adds no code. Met; no code changed. The note works through the planned route first (Option A): the ring carries the access time, either as the drain time or packed into the slot word, and the policy rewrites the entry's deadline. It rejects that route for three reasons. The ring drops reads by design: M2.8c's probe drained 33_796 of 6_544_614 reads/s under four readers, so a key read 100 times in one idle window would be expired while in use about 59% of the time. The maintenance process would have to rewrite live entries, and a compare-and-swap on the id scans the whole table for a key that does not bind a match head (specs/07 §Decisions M4.4b). And every sampled read would move an index row. It recommends Option B: a signed `:atomics` word per entry, at `band(id, words - 1) + 1`. The writer stamps it before the insert, every hit stamps it with `exchange`, and the sweep moves a row that is due but was read since, instead of removing it. Collisions make errors late, never early. The cost is one op on the hit of a cache with the option, so its trigger hit has four ops, past specs/00 goal 1's three. That is open question 1 of specs/11, for a human, along with A1/A2 for such a cache, per-entry idle times and the array size for unbounded caches. specs/00's non-goal now points to specs/11 instead of saying "via the read buffer" (specs/00 §Decisions M6.4). specs/06's intro points to it too. The note's hit-path estimate (20–55 ns over today's bounded hit with one reader) is unmeasured, and so is the Option A pass cost. The Caffeine details came from knowledge of its source, not from a check against it.

## M7 Write-path tuning (added 2026-11-12)

Added by hand after a profile of the maintenance process on the development machine. The write targets are met (the burst check passes and sustained throughput is reported only), so this milestone is about headroom, not a gate. Every task here must leave the cache's behaviour unchanged:

- `mix bench hitrate` reproduces every row to the exact hit. The bench is deterministic (specs/09 §Decisions M2.11e), so any change to a hit count is a behaviour change and a failure of the task.
- `mix bench maintenance` stays 4/4: the A5 bound and the pass budget hold.
- No change to `get/2`, `put/4` or anything else on the hot path of AGENTS.md. This milestone works only inside `Theine.Policy`, `Theine.Maintenance` and `Theine.Sketch`.

**The profile** (2026-11-12, `+sbt db +sbwt very_long`, a full cache with `max_size: 100_000`, one writer for 5 s; `:tprof` call counts on the maintenance process, and each operation's cost timed untraced in a compiled loop, since tracing slowed the process about 2.5×):

| per write event | overwrite of an existing key | new key that evicts one |
|---|---|---|
| total, untraced | ≈ 4.2 µs (≈ 240_000/s) | ≈ 10.5 µs (≈ 95_000/s) |
| ETS ops on the private policy tables, ≈ 250 ns each | 7 ≈ 2.2 µs | 22 ≈ 5.5 µs |
| sketch `increment`/`frequency`, ≈ 440 ns each, of which ≈ 250 ns is the hash | 1 ≈ 0.44 µs | 3 ≈ 1.3 µs |
| one-field struct updates (below) | ~2 ≈ 0.3 µs | ~6 ≈ 1 µs |
| receiving the event (the messaging floor of a GenServer that only decrements `pending`) | ≤ 0.56 µs | ≤ 0.56 µs |

There is no single hot spot. The costliest single operation is the LRU move in `reorder/6`: an `order` delete plus insert, ≈ 0.94 µs. Two findings shape the tasks below:

- On OTP 28 (erts 16.4) a one-key map update (`%{state | k: v}`, `put_map_exact` with one pair) costs 150–230 ns, while a two-key update costs 30–50 ns. This reproduces in plain Erlang with no theine code. Removing two such updates from the overwrite path (`add_segment/3` with delta 0, and the `evicted` update before `consumed/2`) made overwrites ≈ 5% faster, 4.16 → 3.92 µs per event in three alternating runs.
- Batching events would gain little: `flush/2` in the pass already applies about half the events outside the GenServer callback. It is not a task here.

Raising the write ceiling by a large factor needs the policy split across several maintenance processes. That is a design change for specs/ first, not a task for this milestone.

Order: M7.1, M7.2, M7.3, M7.3b, M7.4. Each later task is measured against the M7.1 baseline and the task before it.

Done: M7.1, M7.2, M7.3 (eviction outcome unmet, recorded), M7.3b, M7.4 (not taken, measured). M7 complete.

- [x] M7.1 Per-event write cost in `mix bench maintenance` **(maintenance)** (specs/09 §`mix bench maintenance`; specs/04): a reported scenario, with no threshold, that measures what one write event costs the maintenance process. A full bounded cache (`max_size: 100_000`, filled and drained before timing), one writer for 5 s, two runs: (a) overwrites of uniformly random existing keys, (b) distinct new keys, each evicting one entry. Report puts/s and µs per event for each, as the mean of 3 runs, in the bench's summary and its JSON result. Document the scenario in `bench/README.md` and specs/09 as reported only, with a dated entry under specs/09 §Decisions. — outcome: `mix bench maintenance` prints both rows and still exits 0 at 4/4; the numbers are recorded under §Benchmark log as M7's baseline (expected near 4.2 µs and 10.5 µs on the development machine).
- [x] M7.2 Fewer one-field struct updates on the event path **(maintenance)** **(policy)** (specs/05 §Events; specs/04 §The pass): on the `on_write`, `on_remove`, `on_access` and `evict` paths, fold one-field `%{state | k: v}` updates into the multi-field update next to them, and return the state unchanged where nothing changes (`add_segment/3` with delta 0). In `Theine.Maintenance`, fold the `evicted` update into `consumed/2`'s update. In `replace/6`, merge the two `:ets.update_element/3` calls on the key's policy row (the segment and seq written by `reorder/6`, and the id, weight and `expires_at` written by `replace/6`) into one. Add a dated entry under specs/05 §Decisions recording the one-key update cost above, so later code on the event path keeps to multi-field updates. — outcome: `mix bench hitrate` identical to the hit; `mix bench maintenance` 4/4; M7.1's overwrite and eviction µs per event both lower than the baseline, recorded under §Benchmark log.
- [x] M7.3 Hash each key for the sketch once **(maintenance)** **(policy)** (specs/02 §Hashing, §Operations; specs/05 §State): keep the key's sketch hash in its policy row, so that `increment` and `frequency` on a key the policy holds skip `:erlang.phash2/2`, the spread and the rehash. `Theine.Sketch` gains variants that take the stored hash, and the hash function is unchanged, so every counter is bit-identical (the sketch's property tests and `positions/2` still hold). The row shape changes, so every `@row_*` position, row pattern, `restore/5` and the test helpers that read rows move with it, and specs/05 §State is updated. The drained reads benefit too: every drained hit increments the sketch through `reorder_and_sample/5`. — outcome: `mix bench hitrate` identical to the hit; `mix bench maintenance` 4/4 with the mixed-run pass p99 no higher than before; M7.1's eviction µs per event lower than M7.2's, recorded under §Benchmark log. Done 2026-11-12 with the eviction outcome unmet (§Benchmark log M7.3, specs/05 §Decisions M7.3): hits identical, 4/4, pass p99 3903.76 µs against 3908.16. The policy probe has an overwrite 0.07 µs and a drained read 0.03 µs faster, and a new key unchanged (6.145 against 6.170 µs). The hash costs 55 ns, not the profile's 250 ns, and `admit?` has no row from which to take a stored hash. The new key's cost is in the `order` walk, which M7.3b takes on.
- [x] M7.3b One tree descent per `order` step **(maintenance)** **(policy)** (specs/05 §State, §`evict()`; added by M7.3, specs/05 §Decisions M7.3): `lru_entry/2` steps with `:ets.next/2`, then reads the key with `:ets.lookup_element/3`, so the `ordered_set` is descended twice, about 380 ns each on 100_000 rows. M7.3 measured this as most of a new key's cost. Use OTP 27's `:ets.next_lookup/2` in `lru_entry/2`, and `:ets.prev_lookup/2` in `mru/2`. In `candidate/1`, one `prev_lookup` from `base + bound + 1` replaces the exact `lookup`, the `prev` and the `lookup_element`, since the cursor's own row is the first row below that key. `previous/2` needs only the key and keeps `:ets.prev/2`. Behaviour is unchanged, so the eviction property tests and every policy test hold as they are. — outcome: `mix bench hitrate` identical to the hit; `mix bench maintenance` 4/4; `bench/results/policy-m73.exs` (gitignored; alternating rounds against M7.3's policy) shows the new-key scenario lower in every round, and the mean is recorded under §Benchmark log. M7.4 then measures against M7.3b. Done 2026-11-12 (§Benchmark log M7.3b, specs/05 §Decisions M7.3b): hits identical on every row, 4/4, new key 6.246 → 6.096 µs, lower in every round. The gain is about 40 ns per descent saved, not 380 ns: the segment ends and the cursor stay in cache. M7.4's target removes a delete at a random `order` key, which does pay the cold descent.
- [x] M7.4 Lazy deletion in the LRU `order` table, kept only if it pays **(maintenance)** **(policy)** (specs/05 §State, §`evict()`): a move inserts the key's new `{base + seq, key}` row and leaves the old one, and the policy row's seq is the authority. Every walk of `order` (`lru_entry`, the candidate cursor and `previous/2`, `demote`, `fitting_lru`, the climber's transfers, the `lru/2` and `mru/2` helpers) drops a row whose seq no longer matches the key's row and moves on. Stale rows must stay bounded: compact in bounded steps inside the pass, inside `pass_budget`, for example when `order` holds more than twice the live rows. A property test drives random event sequences and checks that the live order is the same as with eager deletion. — outcome: `mix bench hitrate` identical to the hit; `mix bench maintenance` 4/4; M7.1's overwrite µs per event at least 0.3 µs lower than M7.3b's. If the gain falls short or the hit counts move, revert the code, record the measurement under §Benchmark log and in a dated specs/05 §Decisions entry, and check the task off as not taken. **Not taken** (2026-11-12; §Benchmark log M7.4, specs/05 §Decisions M7.4). `lib/` is unchanged. A probe measured the gain before any of it was built, because the cheapest version of the design already fails the outcome. It removed the overwrite's `order` delete and put nothing in its place: no validity check and no compaction. That saved 0.67 µs per overwrite (3.028 → 2.363). The overwrite scenario never walks `order`, so all its stale rows have to be compacted. A bounded-step walk that checks each row against `policy` costs 0.793 µs per stale row at a 2× trigger, and 0.624 µs at 3×. That puts the lazy overwrite 0.13 µs slower and 0.01 µs faster than eager deletion. A one-shot rebuild from `policy` reaches a 0.26 µs gain at 3×. That is still short of 0.3, and it takes about 100 ms, 20× `pass_budget`. These figures leave out the per-row check on every walk, so the real gain would be lower still. The overwrite's cost is the cold descent to a random key in a tree, and deferring the delete does not avoid it. No `mix bench hitrate` or `mix bench maintenance` run: no code changed.

## M8 Partitioned maintenance (added 2026-11-12; start after M7 is checked)

specs/12 is the design, and its questions are settled (specs/12 §Settled questions). One maintenance process applies every write event and so caps a cache's sustained writes. M8 splits the policy across `partitions: n` processes over one entries table, with the partition carried in each id (`id &&& (n - 1)`), so a hit routes its read without hashing the key.

Rules for every task here:

- `partitions: 1` stays exact. The suite passes without changes to existing tests, `mix bench hitrate` reproduces every row to the exact hit, and the tracing multisets of specs/10 are unchanged. That includes `put/4` at `n = 1`, which must not hash the key.
- Each task updates the specs it touches, as listed in specs/12 §What a build would change, in the same commit.
- The thresholds in M8.5 come from specs/12 §Settled questions, a human decision, so writing them into specs/09 is not a relaxation.

- [x] M8.1 Ids and routing, `partitions: 1` exact **(hot path)** (specs/12 §The partition of a key, §Ids carry the partition; specs/01 §Write path, §Expired read; specs/03 §Delivery with backpressure): `%Config{}` gains `partitions`, always 1 in this task, not yet a `start_link` option. `Theine.Owner` allocates `ids` and `pending` with one counter per partition. A `Theine.Partition` module has `of_key/2`, the top `log2(n)` bits of `:erlang.phash2(key, 4_294_967_296)` and `0` without hashing at `n = 1`, and `of_id/2`, `id &&& (n - 1)`. `put/4`, `put_new/4` and a loader's commit allocate `:atomics.add_get(ids, p + 1, 1) * n + p`. `Theine.WriteEvents.deliver/2` takes the partition from the event's id and uses that partition's `pending` and maintenance name. Config holds the names as a tuple, partition 0 under today's name — tests: partition_test property "for n in 1, 2, 4, 8 every id allocated for a key has of_id equal to of_key of that key", "a key's ids increase", "of_key at n = 1 calls no hash" (tracing); tracing_test multisets unchanged; `mix bench get` within noise of M7's final gate. — outcome: nothing observable changes at `n = 1`. Done 2026-11-13 (§Benchmark log M8.1). `Theine.Partition` has `of_key/2`, `of_id/2` and `next_id/3`. `of_key/2` computes `(phash2(key, 2^32) * n) >>> 32`, which equals the top `log2(n)` bits for a power-of-two `n`. `next_id/3` has an `n = 1` clause that is the old `add_get` and does not hash. `put/4`, `put_new/4` and the loader's commit, which goes through `Entry.put/4`, all allocate through it. `WriteEvents.deliver/2` has a `partitions: 1` clause, the old path, and an `n > 1` clause that routes by `of_id` to `elem(config.maintenances, p)` and counts at `pending` index `p + 1`. `Config` has `partitions` (1, not an option) and `maintenances`, the names tuple. `Config.maintenance_name/2` gives partition 0 today's name and partition `p` `Theine.Maintenance.<cache>.Partition<p>`. `Config.new!/1` now validates `name` first, since the names derive from it. The Owner allocates `ids` and `pending` with `partitions` counters. M8.2 owns the rest of the routing: `Maintenance` still reads `pending` index 1, and `ReadBuffer.schedule_drain/1` still uses `config.maintenance`. Tests: test/theine/partition_test.exs. It has the three named tests. "of_key at n = 1 calls no hash" traces `:erlang` and checks that the same trace does see `phash2` at `n = 2`. A second trace shows that `put`, `put_new` and `delete` on a running cache call no `phash2`. It also has a property that `Entry.put/4` and `put_new/4` on a hand-built partitioned config store ids of the key's partition, and an `of_key` property against the top-bits definition. A test with per-partition forwarders shows that put, expire, delete, put_new and the expired read each reach the key's partition and count in its `pending`. Other tests cover per-partition backpressure (the call goes only to the full partition), unique per-writer-increasing ids from concurrent writers, and the Owner's counter sizes for n in 1, 2, 4 and 8. config_test checks the defaults and the names. No existing test changed, and the suite passes (433 tests with the new ones). The tracing multisets are unchanged. `mix gate` is clean, with Loader, Policy, Partition and WriteEvents at 100.00%. `mix bench get --dev-floor` exited 0 (78 pass, 12 floor, 0 fail). A `--sizes 1000 --scenarios put_burst` rerun put the one high burst row (9.02×) back at 6.93×.
- [x] M8.2 `n` maintenance processes for unbounded caches **(maintenance)** (specs/12 §Supervision, §Restart, §A5 with partitions; specs/04 §Restart semantics; specs/08 §Supervision tree, options table): `partitions` becomes a `start_link` option, a power of two from 1 to 64, default 1, else `ArgumentError`. In this task `n > 1` is accepted only with `max_size: :infinity`. A finite `max_size` with `n > 1` raises `ArgumentError` naming M8.4's limitation, and M8.4 lifts it. With `n > 1`, `Theine.Partitions` (`one_for_one`) supervises `n` `Theine.Maintenance` processes. Each has its own policy and ids tables, expiry index, `pending` with backpressure at `write_hwm`, and `sizes` fields. Its restart rebuild selects the rows with `band(id, n - 1) == p`. `n = 1` keeps today's tree — tests: supervisor_test "with partitions: 4, killing partition 1 restarts only partition 1" (the other pids and pass counts unchanged); maintenance_test "a restarted partition rebuilds exactly its own keys", "each partition sweeps its own expired keys"; write_events_test "backpressure on one partition does not block a writer of another"; config_test for the option's validation. — outcome: an unbounded cache with `partitions: 4` serves `put`, `get`, `delete` and `expire` over 100_000 keys from 4 writers, and loses no event: once idle, each partition's policy holds exactly the table's keys whose ids belong to it. Done 2026-11-13 (§Benchmark log M8.2; specs/04, 05, 08, 12 §Decisions M8.2). `partitions` is an option after `drain_backoff`. `Config.check_partitions!/1` raises for `n > 1` with a finite `max_size`, naming M8.4, and fills `maintenances`. The config gains `partition`, 0 in the stored term. A maintenance process is started as `{cache, p}` and works on `Config.partition(config, p)`, so `pending` and the drain flag are at `p + 1` and its policy publishes `sizes` from `2p + 1` (`Policy` keeps the index as `size_at`; `Policy.size/2`, `weighted_size/2`). The Owner allocates `n` flags and `2n` size fields. `Theine.Partitions` (`one_for_one`, child ids `{Theine.Maintenance, p}`) is the last child when `n > 1`. The rebuild's match spec gains `{'==', {'band', Id, n - 1}, p}` at `n > 1` only. Partition `p > 0` seeds `:rand` from `{cache, p}` (`rand_seed/2`). Follow-up passes go through the new `ReadBuffer.schedule_drain/2`; the hit path's `schedule_drain/1` is untouched. `Maintenance.info/2` exists, and `run`, `reset`, `pause` and `resume` take an optional partition; `policy_row/2` asks the key's partition. Tests: test/theine/supervisor_test.exs (new: the `n = 1` tree, the `n = 4` tree and names, killing partition 1 restarts only it with the other pids and pass counts unchanged, an Owner kill restarts every partition, stopping unregisters them); maintenance_test "a restarted partition rebuilds exactly its own keys" (partition 2 paused with queued events keeps its `pending` across partition 1's restart), "each partition sweeps its own expired keys", and the outcome test "4 writers' put, get, delete and expire over 100_000 keys lose no event" (about 2 s); write_events_test "backpressure on one partition does not block a writer of another"; config_test for the option and `partition/2`; read_buffer_test for `schedule_drain/2`. config_test's M8.1 test "… is not an option yet" now says the option exists. The suite has 448 tests; the new files passed 20 repeated runs. `mix gate` is clean with Loader, Policy, Maintenance, Partitions and WriteEvents at 100.00%. `mix bench maintenance` 4/4, `mix bench hitrate --traces loop` identical to the hit, stress tier 7/7.
- [x] M8.3 The calls that span partitions (specs/12 §Calls that span partitions; specs/08 §Calls, §Telemetry): `size/1` and `weighted_size/1` sum the partitions' fields. `stats/1` is unchanged. `clear/1` deletes all objects, then calls `reset/1` on each partition in turn. `run_maintenance/1` runs one full pass of each partition in order. `Theine.Maintenance.info/2` takes a partition, and `info/1` is partition 0's. M8.2 already added `Maintenance.info/2` and the partition argument of `run/2`, `reset/2`, `pause/2` and `resume/2` (default 0), and `Policy.size/2` and `weighted_size/2` read partition `p`'s `sizes` fields, so this task wires the public calls to them. Until it lands, `size/1`, `weighted_size/1`, `clear/1` and `run_maintenance/1` of a partitioned cache act on partition 0 only (specs/08 §Decisions M8.2). `[:theine, :maintenance, :pass]` and `:rebuild` carry `partition: p` in their metadata, at `n = 1` too — tests: api_test "size/1 of a partitioned cache equals the table size once idle", "clear/1 empties every partition and size/1 reads 0", "run_maintenance/1 runs a pass on each partition"; telemetry_test "pass and rebuild events carry the partition". — outcome: every call in specs/08 §Calls returns its documented shape on an unbounded `partitions: 4` cache. Done 2026-11-13 (specs/04, 08, 12 §Decisions M8.3). `size/1` and `weighted_size/1` keep a `partitions: 1` clause with today's one-field read and sum `Policy.size/2` or `weighted_size/2` over the partitions otherwise. `clear/1` deletes all objects and then calls `Maintenance.reset/2` on partitions 0 to `n - 1`. `run_maintenance/1` calls `Maintenance.run/2` the same way. The pass and rebuild metadata is `%{cache: name, partition: p}`. `Maintenance.info/1` stays partition 0's. There is no api_test or telemetry_test file, so the API tests went into test/theine_test.exs (describe "partitions: 4": the three named tests and "every call of specs/08 §Calls returns its documented shape", which covers get/2 and /3, put, put_new, fetch/2 and /4, fetch_async, fetch_all, expire, persist, ttl, delete, refresh with `:refreshing`, size, weighted_size, stats, run_maintenance and clear), and the telemetry test went into maintenance_test's partitions describe ("pass and rebuild events carry the partition, at n = 1 too": the metadata has exactly the two keys at `n = 1`, each partition's pass counts only its own writes, and a killed partition 2's rebuild names partition 2 and counts its own entries). No existing test changed. The suite has 453 tests, the touched files passed 30 repeated runs, `mix gate` is clean with Loader, Policy, Maintenance, Partition, Partitions and WriteEvents at 100.00%, and the stress tier passed 7/7. No bench: the task is not marked, and the hot path and the pass are unchanged apart from one metadata key.
- [x] M8.4 Bounded partitions **(hot path)** **(maintenance)** **(policy)** (specs/12 §The read buffer is split by partition, §Each partition is a whole policy, §A5 with partitions, §Settled questions 1; specs/03 §Layout, `schedule_drain/1`, §Hits from the tails; specs/02 §Layout; specs/05 §Regions and sizes): the read buffer holds `n` groups of `stripes`. A partitioned cache gets its own `hit` shape, and a hit records into its id's group. `n = 1` keeps the 5-tuple and its clause. Each partition has its own drain flag and drains only its own group, and `hits` sums every tail. Partition `p` gets its capacity share, `div(max_size, n)` plus one for `p < rem(max_size, n)`, with a sketch sized for that share. `start_link` raises `ArgumentError` when `n > 1` and `div(max_size, n) < 10_000`. An entry heavier than its partition's share is never stored. M8.2's bounded restriction is lifted (`Config.check_partitions!/1`). What M8.2 left for this task: a reader's trigger still calls `ReadBuffer.schedule_drain/1`, partition 0's flag and process, and must call `schedule_drain/2` with its id's partition (the Owner already allocates `n` flags at index `p + 1`); `Maintenance.init/1` calls `ReadBuffer.reset_heads/1` over every stripe and must reset only its own group; the Owner allocates one `Sketch.new(max_size)`, and `info/2`'s `read_buffer` and `sketch` must describe the partition's own. Each process already works on `Config.partition(config, p)`, which is where the share and the partition's sketch belong. If this is too big for one iteration, split it here into (a) the read buffer and `hit` shape and (b) the shares — tests: read_buffer_test "a hit records into the stripes of its id's partition", "a partition's drain sees only its own ids"; sketch_test "at n = 8, 100_000 random keys of one partition fill its sketch's blocks evenly (chi-square at 1%)"; config_test "minimum share", "shares add up to max_size"; api_test "an entry heavier than its share is not stored"; tracing_test "the partitioned hit's multiset equals the bounded hit's"; maintenance_test "with partitions: 4 under saturating writers the table never exceeds max_size + 4 * write_hwm + writers". — outcome: `mix bench get`'s `n = 1` rows within noise of M8.1's, `mix bench hitrate` identical to the hit, `mix bench maintenance` 4/4. Done 2026-11-13 (§Benchmark log M8.4; specs/01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 08, 10, 12 §Decisions M8.4). Not split. The read buffer is one array of `stripes * partitions` stripes. `ReadBuffer.drain/3`, `reset_heads/1`, `lag/1` and `dump/1` work on the group of `config.partition` (`ReadBuffer.group/1`), and `hits/1` sums every tail. `record/2` has a `partitions: 1` clause, which is the old body, and an `n > 1` clause. A partitioned cache's `hit` is the 7-tuple `{buffer, stripe_mask, width, slot_mask, trigger_mask, n - 1, stripes * width}`. `get/2` and `refreshing_hit/5` match it after the 5-tuple and call `record_partitioned/3`, whose trigger is `schedule_drain/2` with the id's partition. The Owner allocates one sketch per share into the new runtime field `sketches`; the stored config's `sketch` is nil at `n > 1`. `Config.partition/2` at `n > 1` with a finite `max_size` sets the view's `max_size` to `Partition.share/3` and its `sketch` to `elem(sketches, p)`, so `Policy` and `Maintenance` did not change. `Config.check_partitions!/1` raises below `Config.min_share/0` (10_000) and names the least `max_size` for that `n`. `Entry`'s oversize check uses `Partition.oversized?/4`, which hashes the key only for a weight of exactly `div(max_size, n) + 1`. Tests: config_test "minimum share" (replaces M8.2's restriction test, which this task lifts), property "shares add up to max_size", and the bounded `partition/2` view; partition_test property for `oversized?/4` and a trace that only the boundary weight hashes; read_buffer_test "a hit records into the stripes of its id's partition" (one reader pinned per scheduler), "a partition's drain sees only its own ids" (and `reset_heads/1` of one partition), and the per-partition trigger; sketch_test chi-square at 1% for all eight partitions at n = 8, and the same keys in a whole-cache sketch; theine_test (no api_test file) describe "bounded partitions": "an entry heavier than its share is not stored", each partition evicts to its share and has its own sketch and stripes, a hit records into its id's group with `stats/1` summing every group, and the refresh hit; tracing_test "the partitioned hit's multiset equals the bounded hit's" (also over `:erlang`), the partitioned trigger hit, and the partitioned put and delete multisets (one `phash2` on the put); maintenance_test "with partitions: 4 under saturating writers the table never exceeds max_size + 4 * write_hwm + writers" (and each policy within one event of its share), and "a restarted bounded partition resets only its own stripes and its own sketch". The suite has 473 tests. `mix gate` is clean, with Loader, Policy, Maintenance, Owner, Partition, Partitions, ReadBuffer and WriteEvents at 100.00%. `mix bench maintenance` 4/4. `mix bench get --dev-floor` failed only the deferred A1 row (open question 4), on binary_1kb in the full run and on tuple3 in the rerun. `mix bench hitrate`, run in two parts (19/19 and 6/6), matched M7.3b to the hit on every `theine` and `theine_static` row. The gate was re-run on the final tree: clean, 473 tests. The stress tier passed 7/7.
- [ ] M8.5 Bench subjects and thresholds **(maintenance)** **(policy)** (specs/12 §Acceptance, §Settled questions 2–5; specs/09): add `theine_p4` (`partitions: 4`) to `mix bench hitrate` on DS1, S3, P3 and zipf 100_000, within 1 point of `theine` on each row. Add `theine_p4` to `mix bench get`, with p50 at most 1.10 × `theine`'s on every shape. Add an overshoot run with `partitions: 4` to `mix bench maintenance`, with the bound `max_size + 4 * write_hwm + schedulers`. Run M7.1's per-event scenario at `partitions` 1, 2 and 4 with one writer per scheduler; this is reported on the development machine. Record specs/12's reference-machine gate (`partitions: 4` ≥ 2 × `partitions: 1`) in specs/09 §Reference machine and as a human row under §Final gate. specs/09 gets these thresholds with a dated decision entry citing specs/12 §Settled questions. — outcome: all three benches exit 0 with the new rows, recorded under §Benchmark log.
- [ ] M8.6 Docs, README and specs/00 (specs/12 §What a build would change; specs/08 §Documentation): document `partitions` in the `start_link/1` doc: when to use it, the minimum share, the entry-weight limit, and the A5 slack. In the README, the paragraph on sustained writes points to `partitions` instead of hand sharding, and the benchmarks section gains the throughput rows. Move partitioned maintenance out of specs/00 §Non-goals into the description of the cache, with A5's slack for `n > 1`. Mark specs/12 as built. — outcome: `mix docs` builds without warnings, and the README, specs/00 and specs/12 agree.

## Discovered issues


1. **`nebulex_local` availability** (resolved in M5.1). Nebulex 3 splits the local adapter into its own package: `nebulex_local` 3.1.0 pairs with `nebulex` 3.1.0 on Hex (both 2026-10-18), and both are dev-only deps.
2. **Concurrent writes to one key can leave the policy with the wrong id** (found in M1.2, resolved in M1.5). `put/4` allocates the id before the insert, and the write event is sent after it, so two writers racing on one key can produce any interleaving of (insert, send) pairs: the entries table may end with the lower id while the policy applies the higher id's event last, or vice versa. The spec's rules (last `:put` event wins; `on_remove` ignores a stale id) then left a policy row whose id never matched the table; in an unbounded cache `size/1` drifted by one and `delete/2` could not clear the row. Resolved as recommended (specs/05 §Decisions 2026-10-27 M1.5): `on_remove(key, id)` with a non-matching id does one `:ets.member/2` on the entries table and drops the row when the key is absent; when the key is present with a third id, the row stays and that id's `:put` corrects it. Tested in policy_test, maintenance_test and the 200-round race in theine_test. Since M3.2 a lower id's `:put` arriving after a higher id's is applied only while the table holds it, so one direction of the reorder no longer leaves the row on a stale id (specs/05 §Decisions M3.2).
3. **A write that straddles `clear/1` can leave a policy row with no entry** (noted in M1.5, not fixed). A writer that inserted before `:ets.delete_all_objects/1` and whose `:put` event is consumed after `Maintenance.reset/1` re-adds a policy row for a key the table no longer holds. No later event for that key arrives on its own (a `delete/2` of an absent key sends nothing), so in an unbounded cache the row lives until a `put` of the same key or a Maintenance restart rebuild (M2.9). In a bounded cache the eviction re-lookup drops it. The window is a few microseconds per `clear/1` and the cost is one phantom entry in `size/1`; the spec accepts it (specs/01 §Write path `clear/1`). If it matters later, `reset/1` could record the `ids` high-water mark and `on_write` ignore `:put` events with a lower id until the table confirms the key.

4. **The `ets` p99 floor at n=1000 tuple3 parallel=1 is noisy enough to fail A1 on its own** (M1.5, M1.6, M2.3). Four crossings of the 1.5× p99 limit by `theine_unbounded` at that one shape with no hit-path regression (M2.3 did not touch `get/2` at all and crossed at 1.51× over a 362 ns floor), each cleared by a rerun of the same tree. The procedure (rerun `--sizes 1000`, pass there clears it) is in specs/09 §Decisions M1.6 and AGENTS.md; the thresholds are unchanged. If a later hot-path task (M2.8, M4.4) keeps hitting it, a bench-only task could measure the `ets` floor twice per shape, before and after the theine subjects, and report the shape's ratios against the floor measured closest in time; that changes the harness, not the limit, and would need a specs/09 Decisions entry. M5.1b (2026-11-03, `lib/` unchanged) saw the bounded `theine` p99 at n=1000 parallel 1 past the 1.65 development band of specs/09 §Reference machine in two runs of the same tree. The full run had binary_1kb at 1.98 and tuple3 at 1.47. The `--sizes 1000` rerun had binary_1kb at 1.44 and tuple3 at 1.73. `theine_nostats` moved with it (1.76, then 1.73). So M5.1c's `--dev-floor` run can report `fail` on this row with no regression. The M1.6 rerun procedure still applies, and the band is not widened. M5.1c bore it out. It made three `--dev-floor` runs of one tree, and the row left the band in two of them, on one value kind each time: tuple3 1.90 (binary_1kb 1.46), then binary_1kb 1.66 (tuple3 1.39), then neither (1.46, 1.37). Counting M5.1b, the bounded p99 at n=1000 parallel 1 was past 1.65 in three of five runs. Both sides of the ratio move: `ets` p99 at tuple3 was 361, 460 and 420 ns, and `theine`'s binary_1kb p99 was 618, 692 and 646 ns. M5.6 should expect a rerun. The bench-only lever above (the `ets` floor measured beside each theine subject, or the p99 checks taken over several runs) would need a specs/09 §Decisions entry. It must not loosen the check. Nothing else in the band is close: in the full run the next-worst floor row was 1.61 on a p50. M5.6 (2026-11-03, `lib/` unchanged since M5.3) had the row outside the band in all three of its runs: tuple3 2.02 in the full run (binary_1kb 1.51), then 1.91 and 1.92, then 1.67 and 1.46. `ets` p99 was 363, 366 and 367 ns, and `theine`'s tuple3 p99 was 733, 699 and 613 ns. So the lever above, an `ets` floor measured closer in time, would not help here. The `ets` side is steady and the `theine` tail moves. Counting M5.1b and M5.1c, the row was past 1.65 on at least one value kind in 7 of 8 runs of one `lib/`. It is the gate's one unmet row (M5.6b, open question 4). `theine_nostats` is not checked, but it shows the same tail (1.57–1.91). M5.6b measured it: the tail is in the bounded hit, not in the maintenance passes. With maintenance paused for a whole run the row was past 1.65 in 5 of 10 Benchee rounds, and no `drain_backoff` or read-buffer setting moved it (§Benchmark log M5.6b, open question 4).

5. **A heavier rewrite of a protected key left `protected_weight` above `protected_max`** (found by M2.6's eviction property at run 158 of 3_000, resolved in M2.6). `on_write` of a known key adjusted the segment weight by the delta and moved the key to its segment's MRU, but only `on_access` demoted; a 7-weight protected key rewritten at weight 9 beside another protected key exceeded the 15-weight limit of `max_size` 20. Caffeine's `demoteFromMainProtected` runs at the end of every maintenance; here `on_write` applies the `on_access` rule after a weight increase (demote the protected LRU until it fits, or move a key heavier than `protected_max` to the probation MRU), so the specs/05 §Acceptance invariant now says "after every event" (specs/05 §Events `on_write`, §Decisions M2.6). The M2.5 property had the same exposure at 100 runs and never hit it; the eviction property keeps `max_runs: 1000` (about a second).

6. **The bounded hit path and the maintenance duty cycle both miss the A1/A2 budget** (found in M2.8; (a) resolved in M2.8b, (b) resolved in M2.8c, (c) open for M2.8d). The first full `mix bench get` with the bounded subject gave `theine` p50 at 1.64–1.98× ets and ips at 0.39–0.52× at parallel 4 (table under §Benchmark log, M2.8). Two separate causes, measured in compiled loops with `+sbt db +sbwt very_long`: (a) the spec's `record/2` costs four `:atomics` ops on a full ring (add_get, get, compare_exchange through `schedule_drain/1`, plus the remote call) at 18–25 ns per op, about 105 ns over the unbounded hit, where specs/01 §Decisions had budgeted about 65; and (b) the maintenance process runs passes back to back under read load (98% busy with one reader at 1.7 µs per `on_access` through the private ETS tables), so at full parallelism the readers share three of four schedulers. Neither is fixable inside `get/2` alone; the first wants a cheaper ring protocol (specs/03), the second a rate limit on drain-triggered passes (specs/04). The unbounded subject is unaffected once the hit path reads a single config key. M2.8b took (a) from 105 ns to about 60 ns over the unbounded hit with a two-op ring and power-of-two masks (specs/03 §Decisions M2.8b); a third of the saving came from replacing three `rem` divisions with `band`, which is worth remembering for M4.4's refresh check. M2.8c took (b) from 99% busy to 21% with the `drain_backoff` rate limit (specs/04 §Decisions M2.8c), and the bounded p99 at parallel 1 from 1.56–2.32× to 1.05–1.57×. Two things it measured are worth keeping: under four saturating readers a pass of 256 reads takes 1.6 ms of wall time (6 µs per `on_access`, against 1.7 µs with one reader), because the maintenance process shares a scheduler with a reader and its wall-time duration includes being descheduled, which M2.11's `pass_budget` measurement must expect; and `k` 7 against 3 gains about 1% of reads/s, so the knob is not the lever for what remains. (c) What remains is the hit path: at parallel 4 the bounded subject runs at 0.86–0.90× of `theine_unbounded`, which itself sits at 0.67–0.72× `ets` on the tuple3 shapes, so A2 fails there (0.57–0.62×) and passes on the binary_1kb shapes (0.71–0.72×); and at parallel 1 the bounded overhead over the unbounded hit grows with n (52, 91, 99 ns at n = 1000, 100000, 1000000) where the two-op protocol should be constant. M2.8d measured the growth and tried the layout levers; A2 is not relaxed. (c) is closed as a recorded floor (specs/09 §Decisions M2.8d): with no passes running the bounded hit is a flat 44–56 ns over the unbounded one at every n, the growth is the concurrent maintenance pass, and the ring levers measured 0 ns at one reader and at most 8–25 ns at four, below the bench's wander, so none was adopted. The reference machine turned out to be two cores with SMT (AGENTS.md), where an atomics op under a busy sibling costs about 65 ns, which is why `parallel: 4` on the tuple3 shapes cannot meet A2 with two ops however they are laid out. (d) The growth itself is the pass's memory traffic through the private policy tables and the sketch, which share the L3 with the reader and grow with n; it survives putting the maintenance process on the other core, and halving the pass rate (`drain_backoff` 7) halves it. That is a maintenance-side lever, M2.11b. (d) is closed as a recorded floor (specs/04 §Decisions M2.11b), and its premise was wrong. With `+S 2` the maintenance process was not on the other core: it ran on the pinned reader's own scheduler. So the growth is the pass's CPU time taken from that reader, and it grows with n because each drained read costs more in larger tables. M2.11b cut that cost by about a quarter (integer `order` keys, a bignum-free sketch hash, fewer struct updates). The gap went from 42–54 ns to 37–38 ns; 20 ns would need the cost halved again.

8. **A pass applies more write events than it can evict, so the table grows past the A5 bound under saturating writes** (found in M2.11; the A5 part resolved in M2.11c by eviction on arrival, specs/04 §Decisions M2.11c; the pass budget part resolved in M2.11d by capping the flush at `write_batch`, specs/04 §Decisions M2.11d). The write-count trigger runs a pass after `write_batch` (256) applied events, and that pass's step 1 flushes up to `write_batch * 4` (1024) more before step 5 evicts at most `evict_batch` (1024): under writers that keep the mailbox full every pass applies 1280 and evicts 1024, and the policy's weighted size grows by 256 per pass with nothing to stop it but the deferred `:drain` follow-ups (which also flush). Measured with `bench/results/overshoot-m211.exs` at `max_size` 10_000: the policy at 3× `max_size` after 2 s while `pending` sat at its 1028 bound; at 1_000_000 through `mix bench maintenance`, a worst sample 156 k over the bound and 128 k still over a second after the writers stopped (§Benchmark log M2.11). Even with the eviction cap raised the bound as specs/03 states it (`max_size` + `write_hwm` + in-flight inserts) cannot hold with batched eviction, because every event applied but not yet evicted is outside `pending` and inside the table, and the sampler sees the pass mid-way; it holds by construction only if an event is not consumed (its `pending` decrement) until the cache fits again, which means evicting as each `:put` is applied (Caffeine's writer runs `maintenance` inline when its buffer is full, which is the same idea). A prototype of that (one `evict/1` after each `on_write`, `pending` decremented after it) passed both A5 checks at 1_000_000 with the worst sample exactly at the bound and 10% more write throughput, and leaves the pass's step 5 a no-op except after a rebuild. It changes when evictions happen relative to a batch's reads (writes are already applied ahead of the pass's reads, so the eviction moves ahead of them too; with one access per pass, Caffeine's simulator order, nothing changes), so it is a policy task that needs `mix bench hitrate` and decisions in specs/03 (what "consumed" means), specs/04 (§The pass: eviction on arrival, the `evicted` measurement as a since-last-pass count) and specs/05 (`evict()` "called once in every pass"). Separately, the pass budget fails in the mixed run with or without the fix (p99 8.5–8.9 ms, max 12.7–21.3 ms at 1_000_000) because a saturated process always has about 1000 events to flush and each costs about 7 µs with its eviction under four workers on the two-core reference machine; the flush cap is the lever (M2.11d), since events are applied on arrival anyway and the flush exists only so that step 3 sees a current policy.

9. **The replay-determinism test is flaky under full-suite load** (found in M2.11d, resolved in M2.11e: the cause was confirmed, and both replay drivers now wait for each miss's put and its eviction with a `Maintenance.info/1` round trip, specs/09 §Decisions M2.11e). Repeating the suite while gating M2.11d turned up three flakes. Two were test bugs and are fixed in M2.11d. In maintenance_test, `attach_pass_handler/1` forwarded every cache's pass events, so a `refute_received` caught another async test's pass; the handler now matches the cache. In policy_test, the setup's named table could still exist when the next test started, because the runtime frees a dead owner's tables asynchronously; `on_exit` now waits for it. The third is theine_test's replay test, at about 1 in 120 full-suite runs, with both replays at the same 626 requested passes but different hits. It is unrelated to the flush cap: its passes see at most 32 events. The suspected cause is M2.11c's eviction on arrival, which makes the eviction's timing relative to the replay's reads depend on scheduling. If that is right, `mix bench hitrate` replays are exposed too. M2.11e confirms and fixes it.

7. **The A3 OLTP rows cannot pass as specs/09 states them** (found in M2.10; resolved 2026-10-29 by a human decision, open question 1: the margin is on `loop` only and the adaptive window, M2.13, carries `theine ≥ lru` on OLTP; met in M2.13 at +6.94, +2.97 and +1.51 points, §Benchmark log M2.13). specs/09 puts the +10-point margin on `loop` 1_000 and on `OLTP` 1_000 and 2_000, and asks `theine` ≥ `lru` on every row. specs/00 A3, which specs/09 derives from, puts the margin on "the looping and scan traces", and specs/09's own table types OLTP as "database". OLTP is the recency-biased trace that Caffeine's authors use to motivate the adaptive window (M6.1): a static 1% window W-TinyLFU sits at LRU's level there and trails it at larger sizes, which is what the harness measures with the production policy: +3.61, +0.18 and −1.01 points at 1_000, 2_000 and 5_000 with the 32-access batches, +2.61, −0.52 and −1.24 replayed one access per pass as Caffeine's simulator does (the batching helps slightly: a hit key is reordered after the batch's inserts). The harness's `lru` reproduces the ARC paper's own LRU figures for OLTP exactly (32.83, 42.47, 53.65 at 1_000, 2_000, 5_000), and that paper's ARC reaches 38.93, 46.08 and 55.25 there; a +10 margin at 1_000 would need 42.8%, above ARC. The window sweep shows the policy reacting as it should to a recency-biased trace (a 20% window adds 4.9, 4.4 and 2.7 points over the 1% window: +8.5, +4.6 and +1.7 over LRU, still short of +10), and `loop` shows the admission filter working (58.84% against LRU 0). The loop may not relax a specs/09 threshold (PROMPT_build.md step 5), so the rows stand, `mix bench hitrate` exits non-zero on them, and the decision is a human's.

10. **The mixed-run pass p99 is within 1 ms of `pass_budget` since the sweep landed** (found in M3.1, open; not a failure). `mix bench maintenance` passes 4/4 with p99 at 3.99 and 4.17 ms against the 5 ms budget, up from 2.85–2.90 ms (§Benchmark log M3.1). That is the index insert on every TTL write and up to `expire_batch` sweep removals per pass. The split between them has not been measured. A later maintenance task (M3.2, M6.2) that pushes the p99 over the budget should first split the pass by step, as M2.11d did (`bench/results/pass-steps-m211d.exs`), before it changes anything. specs/04 §Budget's lever is the cap: a lower `expire_batch` default (1024 now), re-measured. The threshold stays where it is. In M7.1 (2026-11-12, maintenance code unchanged since M6.2) two of three runs missed both budget checks (p99 8.68 and 7.84 ms, max 26.1 and 23.2 ms) while the machine was slow: the mixed run served 7.9 M and 9.5 M reads against 11.8 M in the third run, which passed at p99 4.10 ms (§Benchmark log M7.1). Pass durations are wall time, so a slow machine inflates them. A maintenance task that fails the budget should compare the mixed run's read count with M6.2's (about 12 M) before reading the miss as a regression.

11. **maintenance_test "a sketch wider than reset_chunk resets one chunk per pass" failed once** (found in M3.2, open). It failed in about 120 full-suite runs with a pattern-match failure on pinned variables, so it is one of the `Maintenance.info/1` matches on `increments` or `reset`. The output was not kept, and 100 further runs did not reproduce it. The test's events never reach M3.2's new branches: one sender, ids rising, no removals, no expiry. A likely cause is a pass the test did not request, started by a write-count trigger, a `:drain`, or a writer blocked above `write_hwm`, running the reset before the first `info/1`. Next time: run `mix test test/theine/maintenance_test.exs --repeat-until-failure 500` and keep the failing match. On 2026-10-30 that run, made while every plan task was blocked, did not reproduce it in 358 runs. It stopped at run 358 on a different test's flake, issue 14. A reading of every pass trigger (interval, `:drain`, write-count, `:more`, rebuild) and of `sketch_size` (counted once per applied event in every branch) found no unrequested pass and no drift in this test.

14. **The saturating-writers A5 test checked a bound its sampling could not support** (found and resolved 2026-10-30). maintenance_test "under saturating writers the table never exceeds max_size + pending + writers at any of 200 samples" failed once in 358 runs of the file: left 10992, right 10990. The spec's own check on the same sample, `table <= max_size + write_hwm + writers`, held at 10992 against 11028. The test read `pending` before and after the table and took the larger value. Its comment said the backpressure band (pending in `write_hwm..write_hwm + writers`) kept a peak between the reads within that value. But this sample's `pending` was 988, below `write_hwm`, so the band did not apply. With the sampler descheduled between reads, `pending` can rise and fall back unseen. Within the band the argument is also short by up to `writers`, so a sound version would be weaker than the spec's check. The test now checks the M2.11c invariant directly: `Policy.weighted_size/1`, whose atomics only the maintenance process writes, is at most `max_size + 1` at every sample (one weight-1 event between its `on_write` and its eviction). It keeps the spec's table check. Removing the eviction from `apply_event/2` fails the new check at 10011 against 10001, a value the table check alone would pass. `lib/` unchanged; A5 itself never failed.

12. **A fetcher could win a fresh claim after another claimant committed, and load again** (found and resolved in M4.2, specs/07 §Decisions M4.2). Step 1 claimed with `insert_new` and loaded without checking the table again. A fetcher whose `get` missed while a load ran, and whose `insert_new` ran after that claimant had committed and released, ran the loader a second time. Racing never found it: 300 rounds of 500 fetchers had no double load, because the fetcher must be descheduled within a window of a few calls. The `:claim` pause point hits it every time. M4.5's soak would probably not have caught it either. The fix is one non-counting `:ets.lookup` per claimed miss.

13. **A refresh task killed from outside leaves its `refreshing` row** (found in M4.4, resolved in M4.4b: the row names its task, and a claim that finds it dead deletes it and claims again, specs/07 §Decisions M4.4b). The task deletes its row in `after`, which a `:kill` (or the task supervisor's restart under `rest_for_one`, which kills its children while Owner and the tables live on) never runs. The stale entry is then served forever, or until it expires, and `refresh/2` returns `{:error, :refreshing}` for that key. The same hole M4.1 closed for a killed claimant. M4.5's no-leak check would not see it: its soak kills nothing.

## Open questions

1. **Resolved 2026-10-29 by a human** (specs/00, 05, 08, 09 §Decisions): the +10 margin is on `loop` 1_000 only; `theine ≥ lru` stays on every row, OLTP included, and is met by the adaptive window, now on by default (M2.13); a `theine_static` subject with `theine ≥ theine_static − 1` on every row keeps the climber honest; the human gate compares `theine` with Caffeine's `HillClimberWindowTinyLfu` (`simple`) and `theine_static` with `WindowTinyLfu`.

2. **Resolved 2026-11-02 by a human** (specs/00, 09, 10 §Decisions): option (c) with k = 10. The put threshold is the caller's cost in bursts of 1_000 below `write_hwm`, ≤ 10 × an ETS insert (M5.1b). Sustained put ips is reported against `ets` and `cachex` with no threshold, and the README states the cap that the one maintenance process puts on sustained writes. No write-path redesign. The same decision moves the A1/A2 verdicts to a reference machine with four physical cores (the owner's Mac, a human step of M5.6). The development laptop runs `mix bench get --dev-floor` against a fixed band (M5.1c, specs/09 §Reference machine). The question as raised:

   **Raised 2026-10-29 in M5.1: the `put` threshold of specs/09 cannot be met by the write path specs/03 prescribes.** specs/09 asks `theine` put ips ≥ 0.5 × `ets` insert ips and prices a write as "an insert, an id allocation and a message send". The isolated `mix bench get` run with the put scenario measured 0.14–0.19× at parallel 1 and 0.03–0.06× at parallel 4 on every shape. Cachex's put was 0.32–0.42× and Nebulex's 0.06–0.12× in the same run. A compiled probe (specs/09 §Decisions M5.1) puts the floor in the design, not the code. One `send/2` of the event costs 290–330 ns, even to a suspended process, against a 145–214 ns insert, so insert plus send alone is 2.4–3.5× an insert. `Theine.put/3` is 4–5× with no consumer running, and 17–21× sustained, because a writer that outruns the one maintenance process blocks above `write_hwm` (specs/03 §Delivery with backpressure, which A5's bound rests on). The threshold stands until a human changes it. Choices:
   - (a) Keep 0.5×. That needs writes that do not each send a message, for example a striped lossless write buffer over ETS or `:atomics` that the maintenance process drains, as Caffeine's write buffer is drained. This rewrites specs/03 §Write events and the A5 argument, and is several tasks, not M5.1b alone.
   - (b) Restate the check as one the design can meet and that still catches a write-path regression. **Recommended:** make put informational against `ets`, and gate it against the rivals: `theine` put ips ≥ `cachex` put ips at parallel 1. Theine was 0.41–0.53× of Cachex there in this run, and 0.10–0.15× at parallel 4, so this check fails today too. Add a task that cuts the maintenance process's cost per write event and the backpressure round trip, measured by `mix bench maintenance`'s write run.
   - (c) Restate it as a caller-cost check in bursts under `write_hwm` (`put` p50 ≤ k × insert p50). This measures what the parenthetical prices, but needs a k from a human. The probe has bursts at 5.8–8.5× with the consumer running.

3. **Resolved 2026-11-02 by a human: MIT confirmed** (specs/00 §Decisions M5.4). The question as raised: **the package license (2026-10-29, M5.4).** No spec names one, and `mix hex.build` refuses to build without `licenses:`. The package is MIT with a `LICENSE` naming Will Gilman (specs/00 §Decisions M5.4). **Recommended:** confirm MIT, or switch to Apache-2.0 (Caffeine's license). Switching changes only `licenses:` in `mix.exs` and the `LICENSE` file, and must happen before the first `mix hex.publish`.

4. **Resolved 2026-11-03 by a human: option (b)** (specs/09 §Decisions 2026-11-03). The reference machine decides the bounded A1 p99 at n=1000 parallel 1. The band stays at 1.65, and the development gate's `fail` on that row alone is recorded as "deferred to the reference machine". If the Mac run passes the row, it is the laptop's floor. If it misses, that is a new open question. The question as raised:

   **Raised 2026-11-03 in M5.6: the bounded A1 p99 at n=1000 parallel 1 lies outside the development band in most runs.** The band of specs/09 §Reference machine is ≤ 1.65 for A1. On this row, M5.6's three `mix bench get --dev-floor` runs measured 2.02, 1.91/1.92 and 1.67, and the row was past 1.65 in 7 of the 8 runs since M5.1b, all with the same `lib/`. Every other A1/A2 row of the gate is `pass` or `floor`. The row is not a regression: M5.1c's last rerun had it at 1.46, and the M1.6 procedure ("a second failure in the targeted run is a regression") was written before the bounded subject existed. The loop may not widen the band. A1 itself is judged on the reference machine, whose run is pending. Choices:
   - (a) Keep the band as it is. M5.6b looks for a lever: the pass running on the reader's own scheduler (specs/04 §Decisions M2.11b) is the likely source of the tail at parallel 1. M6 waits until a `--dev-floor` run exits 0.
   - (b) **Recommended:** let the reference machine's run settle this one row. If the Mac run passes A1 p99 at n=1000 parallel 1, the development machine's `fail` there is recorded as the laptop's floor, as M2.8d recorded the others, and M5.6b is checked with its measurement attached. This needs a specs/09 §Decisions entry by a human, because it changes what the development run's exit status means for one row. The thresholds stay as they are.
   - (c) Widen the A1 band for p99 at parallel 1. This is a human decision about the band. The data puts the row's spread at 1.39–2.02.

   **M5.6b (2026-11-03) ruled out (a)'s lever** (§Benchmark log M5.6b). With the maintenance process paused for the whole run, so that no pass runs, the bounded p99/ets at n=1000 tuple3 parallel 1 was 1.37–2.02 over ten Benchee rounds. Its median was 1.70, and it was past 1.65 in 5 of 10. The default cache gave 1.36–1.95, median 1.65, 4 of 10. A pass does slow the reads it overlaps. But those reads, with the 20 µs after each pass, are 4–5% of the reads above p99, and leaving them out lowers the p99 by 2–3 ns. `drain_backoff` 7 or 15 and the read-buffer sizes stayed within the spread between rounds, and `drain_backoff: 0` made the row worse. The row sits on the band edge because of the bounded hit itself: a p50 ratio of 1.46–1.53 (the two `:atomics` ops M2.8d recorded as this machine's floor) and a slightly wider tail than `ets`. No setting in specs/03 or 04 can get it below the paused level. So (a) would leave M6 waiting on a lucky run, and the choice is between (b), still recommended, and (c). A note for (b): Benchee stops each row at 1_000_000 samples, about 0.5 s of calls here. More samples would narrow the spread between runs but would not move the median off the edge. M6 is no longer blocked: M5.6b is checked with this measurement as its outcome.

Earlier: M0.4b was done by hand on 2026-10-27, so `mix bench hitrate`
runs every trace and policy tasks must run it in full, not with
`--traces loop,zipf`.

Earlier questions: the 15 questions raised when this plan was generated were
resolved in the specs on 2026-10-27 (summary in specs/00 §Decisions); the
tasks above were edited to match. New questions go here with a recommended
resolution, and are settled by editing the spec in the build commit that
needs the answer (PROMPT_build.md step 5).

### M7.4 — lazy deletion in `order`, measured before building, against HEAD 330719e (2026-11-12; not taken, `lib/` unchanged)

`bench/results/lazy-m74.exs` (gitignored), `+sbt db +sbwt very_long`. Each round fills a policy of 100_000 keys (`max_size` 100_000). It then applies `events` overwrites of uniformly random existing keys with a new id, then `evict/1`, the overwrite scenario of `bench/results/policy-m73.exs`. The `base` arm is HEAD's `Theine.Policy`. The `nodel` arm is the working tree's, with `replace/6`'s `:ets.delete(order, …)` removed and nothing in its place. That is the most lazy deletion could save: no validity check and no compaction. The overwrite scenario never walks `order`, so leaving the stale rows unread is still correct here. After each `nodel` run, its `order` table holds 100_000 live rows and `events` stale ones. Two compactions are timed on it, each reported per stale row removed. `walk` steps through every row with `next_lookup/2`, reads the key's seq with `lookup_element/3` on `policy`, and deletes the row on a mismatch. This is the bounded-step form. `rebuild` fills a fresh `ordered_set` from a `foldl/3` over `policy`. It is not bounded, and it is a lower bound for any compaction that reads `policy`. Each overwrite leaves exactly one stale row, so the lazy cost per overwrite is `nodel` + compaction.

| stale rows at compaction | rounds | base µs | nodel µs | walk µs/stale | rebuild µs/stale | lazy (walk) gain µs | lazy (rebuild) gain µs | upper bound µs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 100_000 (2 × live) | 6 | 3.028 (2.982–3.077) | 2.363 (2.328–2.396) | 0.793 | 0.899 | −0.128 | −0.233 | 0.665 |
| 300_000 (3 × live) | 3 | 3.079 | 2.463 | 0.624 | 0.352 | −0.008 | 0.264 | 0.616 |

The threshold is a gain of at least 0.3 µs. A bounded compaction gains nothing at either trigger. The one-shot rebuild gains 0.26 µs at 3×, with `order` at four times its live size, and it takes 0.352 µs × 300_000 ≈ 105 ms, against a 5 ms `pass_budget`. The delete itself is worth 0.62–0.67 µs: the cold descent to a random key that specs/05 §Decisions M7.3b predicted. Compaction has to find each stale row again and check it against `policy`, which costs more than the delete it defers. `nodel` also gets slower as the tree grows (2.363 → 2.463 µs).

**Per-event cost, policy only.** `bench/results/policy-m73.exs` (gitignored), six alternating rounds against the M7.3 policy (HEAD), 300_000 events each, `+sbt db +sbwt very_long`:

| scenario | M7.3 mean µs | M7.3b mean µs | M7.3 range | M7.3b range |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| overwrite | 3.097 | 3.128 | 3.026–3.171 | 3.110–3.153 |
| drained read | 2.353 | 2.358 | 2.313–2.376 | 2.340–2.368 |
| new key, evicting one | 6.246 | 6.096 | 6.214–6.342 | 6.069–6.138 |

The new key was lower in every round: M7.3b's slowest round is below M7.3's fastest. The overwrite and the drained read do not walk `order` and stay within each other's spread. The new key saves four descents (window LRU, probation LRU, and two in the candidate lookup at a fresh cursor), so the gain is about 40 ns per descent, not the 380 ns that M7.3 timed for a random key. The ends of a segment and the cursor are descended on every event, so their path stays in cache (specs/05 §Decisions M7.3b).

**Primitive costs**, `bench/results/hash-m73.exs` (gitignored), compiled loop, integer keys, a 100_000-row private `:set` and `:ordered_set` and a 100_000-word sketch, three rounds that agree to a few ns: `Sketch.hash/1` 55 ns; `increment/2` 400 ns, `increment_hashed/2` 330 ns; `frequency/2` 395 ns, `frequency_hashed/2` 315 ns; `lookup_element` on the policy `:set` 203 ns, a whole-row `lookup` 233 ns; `lookup_element` on the `order` `:ordered_set` 378 ns. The M7 profile put the hash at about 250 ns. It is 55 ns on an integer key, and a stored hash is worth having only where its row is already in hand.

**Per-event cost, policy only.** `bench/results/policy-m73.exs` (gitignored) is M7.2's probe plus a drained-read scenario (`on_access/2` of a random key's current id). Six alternating rounds against the M7.2 policy (HEAD), 300_000 events each, `+sbt db +sbwt very_long`:

| scenario | M7.2 mean µs | M7.3 mean µs | M7.2 range | M7.3 range |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| overwrite | 3.133 | 3.065 | 3.105–3.164 | 3.037–3.099 |
| drained read | 2.366 | 2.337 | 2.363–2.370 | 2.318–2.356 |
| new key, evicting one | 6.145 | 6.170 | 6.085–6.226 | 6.147–6.232 |

The overwrite and the drained read were faster in every round. The new key did not move, and **the task's eviction outcome is not met**. That path hashes the new key once, as before, and `admit?` still hashes the candidate and the victim, because reading their rows for a stored hash costs more than hashing (specs/05 §Decisions M7.3). An earlier version routed `frequency/2` through the packed hash and measured 6.17 → 6.26 µs on the new key. So `increment/2` and `frequency/2` keep their direct form (specs/02 §Decisions M7.3). The new key's cost is mostly the `order` walk, which M7.3b takes on.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1001028 | 3477097 | 9339 | 1916.64 | 3903.76 | 11222.82 | 12520078 | 3.73 | 9.61 |

The mixed-run pass p99 is 3903.76 µs against M7.2's 3908.16, and 12.5 M mixed reads show a machine at full speed (discovered issue 10). The per-event overwrite row is below M7.2's 4.12. The new-key row, 9.61 against 7.84, falls in the spread M7.1 measured between invocations of that wall-clock run (8.24–13.35). The policy probe above, which M7.1 named as the comparison, shows no change on that path.

### M7.2 — `mix bench hitrate` (full, in two runs), `mix bench maintenance` and two compiled probes, tree of the M7.2 commit (2026-11-12; hitrate 19/19 and 6/6 with every row identical to the hit, maintenance 4/4)

**Hits.** The non-DS1 run's adaptive `theine` rows were compared with a same-session baseline: `bench/results/hits-m72.exs` (gitignored) compiles a revision's `Theine.Policy` (`HITS_BASE`, default HEAD, here b8a824a) as `Theine.BasePolicy` and replays the traces through it with `policy_module`, under the bench's cache names and options and `Theine.Bench.Cache.access/2`. Its rows match the final gate's percentages. `theine_static` keeps the cache names of M2.11e's run, so it is compared with that run's `theine` rows (`hitrate-91115ee.json`), and DS1 with the final gate's `hitrate-7e8712e.json` (`bench/results/compare-hits.exs`):
