(DS1's base columns are the final gate's.) The lru rows are unchanged too. The percentages are those of §Final gate.

**Per-event cost, policy only.** `bench/results/policy-m72.exs` (gitignored) times `on_write/5` then `evict/1` in one process with no messaging, on a policy of 100_000 keys, 300_000 events per round. Each round runs the previous `Theine.Policy` (`POLICY_BASE`, default HEAD) and this one in turn. Six rounds, `+sbt db +sbwt very_long`:

| scenario | base mean µs | M7.2 mean µs | base range | M7.2 range |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| overwrite | 3.266 | 3.109 | 3.239–3.291 | 3.078–3.135 |
| new key, evicting one | 6.199 | 6.127 | 6.181–6.213 | 6.087–6.148 |

M7.2 was faster in every round of both scenarios. Its spread is about 1%, where M7.1's wall-clock `event_run` is bimodal (3.8–4.1 or 5.3–5.8 µs per overwrite from one round to the next, `bench/results/events-m72.exs`), so the probe is the comparison for M7.3 and M7.4. The overwrite saves 0.16 µs, near the profile's 0.24 µs from three alternating runs, and the eviction path 0.07 µs. The new key's remaining struct updates are the insert's, `evict/1`'s opening of a batch and its settling, all multi-key.

**`mix bench maintenance`**, `ERL_FLAGS="+sbt db +sbwt very_long"`, 4/4:

Both per-event rows are below M7's baseline (overwrite 4.25–5.41 µs, new 8.24–8.95 µs, §M7.1), and the mixed run's 12.4 M reads show a machine at full speed (discovered issue 10).

### M7.1 — `mix bench maintenance` with the per-event write cost, three invocations, and a standalone placement probe, tree of the M7.1 commit (2026-11-12; 2/4, 2/4, 4/4; `lib/` unchanged)

`ERL_FLAGS="+sbt db +sbwt very_long"`. Only bench code changed, and the per-event runs start after the gated runs, so the gated rows measure the M6.2 maintenance code (M6.3 added `fetch_all/4` only). The first two invocations ran while the machine was slow, and both missed the pass budget:

| invocation | write-run writes | mixed reads | pass p50 us | pass p99 us | pass max us | overwrite us/event | new us/event | thresholds |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 2858964 | 7926746 | 2727.46 | 8684.55 | 26073.77 | 9.06 | 13.35 | 2/4 |
| 2 | 2035252 | 9475388 | 2331.43 | 7837.39 | 23225.24 | 4.25 | 8.95 | 2/4 |
| 3 | 3399549 | 11778050 | 2064.58 | 4097.18 | 7152.32 | 5.41 | 8.24 | 4/4 |

Invocation 3 in full:

| subject | max_size | limit | max sample | samples | writes | settled size |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| theine | 1000000 | 1001028 | 1001028 | 2648 | 3399549 | 1000000 |

| subject | passes | p50 us | p99 us | max us |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| theine | 8727 | 2064.58 | 4097.18 | 7152.32 |

| subject | reads | writes | call-path writes | fraction |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| theine | 11778050 | 3924714 | 12474 | 0.00 |

| subject | scenario | max_size | writes | puts/s | us per event |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| theine | overwrite | 100000 | 924634 | 184780.29 | 5.41 |
| theine | new | 100000 | 608245 | 121497.52 | 8.24 |

Its three runs per scenario agree to within 3% (overwrite 5.35, 5.47, 5.42; new 8.46, 8.27, 7.98). Between invocations, the overwrite mean moved by 27% (4.25 against 5.41). The cause is not where the writer and the maintenance process run: `bench/results/placement-m71.exs` (gitignored) runs `Theine.Bench.Load.event_run/3` alone, 8 overwrite runs of 3 s per VM, and counts each side's `scheduler_id` (passes from a telemetry handler). Two VMs gave 4.26–4.54 µs per event over 16 runs, with the writer and the passes spread over schedulers 1–3 in every mix. Run after the 60 s of gated load, the scenario measures a warmer, slower machine some of the time.

**M7's baseline**: overwrite 4.25–5.41 µs per event (the standalone probe 4.26–4.54), new key with eviction 8.24–8.95 µs. That is near the profile's 4.2 µs for overwrites and below its 10.5 µs for evictions; the profile timed one 5 s run. M7.2–M7.4 compare against the task before them in the same session, on the standalone probe, before and after the change (the profile's 4.16 → 3.92 came from three alternating runs), and run the full bench for the 4/4 check. A difference smaller than the spread between invocations is not a result.

### M5.6b — the bounded p99 at n=1000 parallel 1, two compiled probes, tree of 95afe8f (2026-11-03; no lever in specs/03 or 04; `lib/` unchanged)

Both probes ran under `ERL_FLAGS="+sbt db +sbwt very_long"` at n=1000 with tuple3 values and the bench's Zipf(1.0) sample, seed 9 + n. Every theine variant is bounded at `max_size: 1000` unless named otherwise. `paused` is the default cache with `Theine.Maintenance.pause/1` (`test_hooks: true`) for the whole run, so no pass runs and the read buffer only fills and overwrites.

`bench/results/benchee-gc-m56b.exs` is Benchee as bench/get.exs calls it: parallel 1, a 2 s warm-up, the key drawn in `before_each`, `--time 1`. Ten rounds, interleaved, with `ets` measured first in each round and every ratio taken against that round's `ets` p99 (339–432 ns):

| subject | p99/ets median | range | rounds past 1.65 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| unbounded | 1.35 | 1.04..1.67 | 1/10 |
| default | 1.65 | 1.36..1.95 | 4/10 |
| paused | 1.70 | 1.37..2.02 | 5/10 |
| `drain_backoff: 0` | 2.14 | 1.91..2.44 | 10/10 |
| `drain_backoff: 7` | 1.54 | 1.28..1.98 | 4/10 |
| `drain_backoff: 15` | 1.49 | 1.35..1.81 | 3/10 |
| `read_buffer_slots: 16` | 1.66 | 1.36..1.83 | 5/10 |
| `read_buffer_slots: 256` | 1.66 | 1.42..1.93 | 5/10 |

The bounded p50 was 352–389 ns in every variant, against 311–313 unbounded and 241–253 `ets`. The default cache ran about 1_000 passes per row. The runtime counted 0.8–0.9 collections per 1_000 samples in the `ets`, unbounded and paused rows, too few to reach the 1% that sets the p99. That count covers every process, the maintenance process included, and it rose to 6–7 in the default rows, which have passes. Benchee stops at its default `max_sample_size` of 1_000_000. That is about 0.5 s of calls at this shape, so bench/get.exs's `--time 10` measures the same window as `--time 1`.

`bench/results/tail-m56b.exs` times every read of one reader pinned to scheduler 1: 2 M reads per row, three rounds. It stores each read's start and latency in `:atomics` and records each pass's end, duration and `scheduler_id` from a pass telemetry handler. It then classes every read above the row's own p99 by what it overlaps. The reader runs with `min_heap_size` 1_000_000. With the default 233-word heap, the reader collected every ~15 hits, because a hit copies the 6-field entry. That put the p99 of every theine variant at ~1.72 µs, a collection, and left `ets` alone (3.8–4.2×). The probe's first run was that artifact. Default cache, three rounds:

| | round 1 | round 2 | round 3 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| p50 / p99 ns (`ets` p99) | 348 / 509 (307) | 348 / 512 (299) | 348 / 498 (290) |
| p99/ets (paused, unbounded) | 1.66 (1.56, 1.50) | 1.71 (1.57, 1.55) | 1.72 (1.75, 1.30) |
| passes on the reader's scheduler / all | 483/483 | 478/478 | 475/475 |
| pass duty, reads overlapping a pass | 5.40%, 0.14% | 5.48%, 0.14% | 5.37%, 0.14% |
| reads above p99: overlapping a pass / ≤ 20 µs after one / neither | 382 / 517 / 19_001 | 399 / 430 / 19_065 | 386 / 486 / 19_046 |
| p99 of the reads that overlap no pass | 507 | 509 | 496 |

So a pass slows the reads it overlaps: 0.14% of all reads carry about 2% of the tail. Every pass ran on the pinned reader's own scheduler, as in M2.11b. Together with the 20 µs after each pass, passes account for 4–5% of the reads above p99. Without the reads that overlap a pass, the p99 is 2–3 ns lower. Across the probe's other variants (median of three rounds), `drain_backoff` 0, 7 and 15 gave 2.17, 1.77 and 1.73. `read_buffer_slots` 16, 256 and 1024 gave 1.69, 1.86 and 1.68, and `drain_threshold` 128 gave 1.74. Default was 1.71 and paused 1.57. Larger slot counts move a larger share of the tail into the passes: 256 slots drain 4× the reads per pass, and 14–18% of the tail reads overlapped one. `drain_backoff: 0` keeps the maintenance process busy 92–97% of the time and costs the row 0.4–0.5.

What it shows: the row's tail is in the bounded hit itself, not in the passes. With no pass at all the p99 ratio lies on both sides of 1.65 (Benchee median 1.70, 5 of 10 rounds past). With passes it is the same within the rounds' spread. The bounded p50 ratio at this shape is 1.46–1.53 (§Final gate), and the bounded hit's tail is wider than `ets`'s: in the Benchee rounds paused p99/p50 was 1.67–2.02, against 1.41–1.73 for `ets`. Together those put the p99 ratio on the band's edge. No `drain_backoff` or read-buffer setting moves the median below the paused level by more than the spread between rounds, and no pass setting can do better than paused. That leaves no lever inside specs/03 or specs/04, so `lib/` is unchanged. What the bounded hit adds over the unbounded one is its two `:atomics` ops, the floor M2.8d recorded (specs/09 §Decisions M2.8d). Open question 4 now carries this for the human.

### M5.1b — `mix bench get` with the `put_burst` scenario, tree of the M5.1b commit (2026-11-03, 79/90 in the full run; all 6 burst checks pass; `lib/` unchanged)

The runs were on the uncommitted tree with `+sbt db +sbwt very_long`, so
every result file is `get-8641c7d.json`, each overwriting the one before.
The full run took 14.5 minutes. It had 11 failures, all of them bounded
`theine` on the get rows: 3 of A1 and 8 of A2. They are the M2.8d floor
that M5.1c's band handles. One of them, the p99 at n=1000 binary_1kb
parallel 1 (1.98), is past the band, and the rerun of `--sizes 1000
--scenarios get` had it at 1.44. In that rerun the tuple3 row of the same
shape was 1.73 instead (discovered issue 4). A `--scenarios put_burst`
run took 44 s and passed 6/6 at 6.03, 6.49, 8.21, 6.57, 5.29 and 5.46,
in the order of the table below. Every `theine_unbounded` check passes.

### M5.1 — `mix bench get` with `cachex`, `nebulex` and the put scenario, and a compiled put probe, tree of the M5.1 commit (2026-10-29, 61/96 in the isolated full run, 64/96 in the first; `lib/` unchanged)

Both runs were on the uncommitted tree, so the result file is
`get-c1f3b06.json`, the second overwriting the first. Both used `+sbt db
+sbwt very_long`. The first run kept all six subjects alive for a whole
shape. Its `theine` p50 at n=1000000 tuple3 parallel 1 was 2.12×, which
fits Nebulex's generation work landing in `theine`'s window. The harness
now populates, measures and tears down one subject at a time (specs/09
§Decisions M5.1), and that row came back at 1.48×. The isolated run took
15 minutes.

The 35 failures are 12 of A1 and 11 of A2 on the get rows, and 12 of put.
The A1 and A2 `ets` failures are the bounded floor of specs/09 §Decisions
M2.8d, at its shapes and ratios. Two of them are `theine_unbounded`
borderline rows: A2 0.64 at n=1000 tuple3 parallel 4 (0.65 in M3.2) and
1.99 against `cachex` at n=1000000 binary_1kb parallel 4. The six bounded
`cachex` failures (1.78–1.92) follow from the same floor. Every
`nebulex` check passes at 5–7×. The 12 put failures are open question 2.

### M4.4 — `mix bench get` and a compiled A/B probe of the hit, refresh clause added to `get/2`, tree of the M4.4 commit (2026-10-29, 49/60 full run, 15/20 in the `--sizes 100000` rerun; probe: no difference)

The runs were on the uncommitted tree, so the file is `get-b570dd5.json`,
with `+sbt db +sbwt very_long`. All 30 `theine_unbounded` checks passed.
The 11 failures are all in the bounded `theine` subject. Seven are the
recorded floor shapes of M4.2 (specs/09 §Decisions M2.8d). Three more are
p50/p99 within 0.03 of the limit (n=1000 binary_1kb p99 parallel 1 at
1.53, p50 parallel 4 at 1.52; n=100000 binary_1kb parallel 4 p50 at
1.52). The last is n=100000 binary_1kb parallel 1 at 1.98/1.89. That
row's `theine` p50 has read 497, 451, 505, 510 and 428 ns in earlier runs,
then 575 ns here and 755 ns in the rerun, with `theine_nostats` (the same
bounded hit) at 499 and 713. `theine_unbounded` reads 10 to 20 ns above
M4.2 on every row (all passing). That is the same drift as the `ets`
floor.

`get/2` gained a clause, so the hit was measured old against new in one VM
(`bench/results/refresh-hit-m44.exs`, gitignored). It compiles the M4.2
body beside the current `Theine.get/2` and times both on the same caches
under `+S 2 +sbt db +sbwt very_long`, giving the median of 8 rounds of 2 M
hits. Unbounded tuple3 1k keys: 301.4 ns old, 301.8 ns new. Bounded tuple3
1k: 360.5 old, 357.4 new. Unbounded 1 KB binaries 128k keys: 445.4 old,
443.9 new. Bounded 1 KB binaries 128k: 534.1 old, 535.0 new. The probe
finds no change, which is the plan's "`mix bench get` unchanged".

### M4.2 — `mix bench get`, `Theine.Entry.peek/2` added, tree of the M4.2 commit (2026-10-29, 52/60 full run)

This is a regression check only. `get/2` and `put/4` are unchanged; the
task added `Entry.peek/2`, which runs only on a claimed miss. The run was
on the uncommitted tree, so the file is `get-bed249a.json`, with
`+sbt db +sbwt very_long`. All 30 `theine_unbounded` checks passed. The 8
failures are all in the bounded `theine` subject, on the same shapes as
M3.2. That is the recorded floor (specs/09 §Decisions M2.8d): A1 p99 n=1000
tuple3 parallel=1 (1.54); A1 p50 and p99 n=1000 tuple3 parallel=4 (1.52,
1.51); A2 n=1000 tuple3 (0.58); A1 p50 and p99 n=100000 tuple3 parallel=1
(1.57, 1.53); A2 n=100000 tuple3 (0.63); A2 n=1000000 tuple3 (0.64).

### M3.1 — `mix bench maintenance`, expiry index and sweep in the pass, tree of the M3.1 commit (2026-10-29, two runs, both exit 0: 4/4)

Ran on the uncommitted tree, so the result file is `maintenance-6f33f92.json`
(overwritten by run 2). The mixed run's writes are half 100 ms TTLs
(specs/09), so the sweep is the new work here. Until this task those
entries stayed in the table until a read found them or eviction took them.
Now each TTL write costs an index insert on arrival and a sweep removal
100 ms later.

| run | max sample (limit 1001028) | samples | writes (write run) | settled size | passes | p50 us | p99 us | max us | reads (mixed) | writes (mixed) | call-path writes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 1001028 | 2633 | 2950777 | 1000000 | 8698 | 2094.66 | 3986.14 | 6341.86 | 11821318 | 3939276 | 12442 |
| 2 | 1001028 | 2644 | 3138365 | 1000000 | 8652 | 2077.41 | 4168.27 | 6232.63 | 11730606 | 3908990 | 12438 |

Against M2.13's two runs, the pass p50 went from 1.55–1.63 ms to
2.08–2.09 ms and the p99 from 2.85–2.90 ms to 3.99–4.17 ms. The p99 is
still under the 5 ms `pass_budget` and the max (6.2–6.3 ms) under 4× it,
but the p99 headroom fell from about 2.1 ms to 0.8–1.0 ms (discovered
issue 10). Mixed-run writes fell by about 10% (3.91–3.94 M against
4.32–4.43 M), and reads by about as much. The write run has no TTLs, and
its throughput is inside the ±15% band of the maintenance bench memory.
A5 holds at the bound in both runs.

Policy and maintenance task. The benches ran on the uncommitted tree, so
the result files carry the parent's sha (`bench/results/hitrate-7bbe3b6.json`,
`maintenance-7bbe3b6.json`, each overwritten by the later run, so the
tables here are the record).

`mix bench hitrate`: with the `theine_static` subject the full run outlasts
the 2-hour limit of a background command, so it ran as `--traces
OLTP,S3,P3,loop,zipf` (44 min, exit 0, 19/19) and then `--traces DS1`
(79 min over four replay passes, exit 0, 6/6), 25/25 in all. Together they cover every row and every threshold of the full
run, which are per row. Default flags, 32 accesses per pass.

The climber does what it is for on the recency-biased OLTP: +4.3 to +2.8
points over the fixed window. At 1000 it is above the ARC paper's ARC
(38.93), and at 2000 and 5000 it is within a point of it (46.08, 55.25). On
the frequency-biased rows it gives back at most 0.71 points (P3), inside
the 1-point allowance. `theine_static` has exactly the hits of M2.11e's
`theine` on every row (`bench/results/compare-static-m213.exs`,
gitignored, against `hitrate-408f736.json`; e.g. OLTP 1000 324531, loop
1000 88942, zipf 100000 7973675, DS1 8000000 30789224; 12/12 rows), so `adaptive: false` changes
no decision.

`mix bench maintenance`, two runs, both 4/4. The baseline is M2.11b's two
runs of the same day (p99 2814.61 and 2899.83). Run 2 falls between them
and run 1 is 4 µs (0.1%) above the higher one, so the pass p99 is no worse
beyond run-to-run noise:

### M2.11b — `mix bench maintenance`, `mix bench hitrate` and the gap probe, tree of the M2.11b commit (2026-10-29)

Maintenance task. The benches ran on the uncommitted tree, so the result
files carry the parent's sha (`bench/results/maintenance-91115ee.json`,
`hitrate-91115ee.json`). `+sbt db +sbwt very_long`, defaults as M2.11d.

`mix bench maintenance`, two runs, both 4/4 thresholds passed:

| run | max sample (limit 1001028) | samples | writes (write run) | settled size | passes | p50 us | p99 us | max us | reads (mixed) | writes (mixed) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 1001028 | 2651 | 3114717 | 1000000 | 11220 | 1498.42 | 2814.61 | 10154.24 | 13670043 | 4554464 |
| 2 | 1001028 | 2628 | 2829667 | 1000000 | 10862 | 1571.73 | 2899.83 | 8498.18 | 13197541 | 4396799 |
| M2.11d run 1 | 1001028 | 2621 | 2477144 | 1000000 | 9167 | 1825.55 | 3337.36 | 5933.32 | 10927831 | 3642030 |

p50 and p99 are 14–16% lower, and the mixed run applies about 25% more
reads and writes in the same 30 s. The max is higher (8.5–10.2 ms against
5.9–6.1, limit 20 ms). Its cause is not measured. One candidate is a
sketch reset chunk: at the higher throughput the mixed run may now reach
the 10 M increments that start a reset at this size, which M2.11d's run
did not.

`mix bench hitrate`, full run: exit non-zero at 11/13. The two failures are
OLTP 2000 and 5000 `theine ≥ lru` (−0.58 and −1.25), as before; they are
M2.13's. Every one of the 24 rows has exactly the hits of M2.11e's run
(`hitrate-408f736.json`), compared field by field with
`bench/results/compare-hits.exs`, because none of the changes alters a
policy decision. For example, OLTP 1000 theine 324531, DS1 8000000 theine
30789224 and loop 1000 theine 88942 are unchanged, so the M2.11e table
above stands for this tree.

`bench/results/policy-m211b.exs`: `Policy.on_access/2` over the bench's
Zipf(1.0) ids and `on_write/5` + `evict/1` of a new key in a full cache, ns
per call, one process pinned to scheduler 2, `+S 2`, each lever added in
turn:

| tree | on_access n=100000 | on_access n=1000000 | write+evict n=100000 | write+evict n=1000000 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| parent (91115ee) | 2412 | 2869 | 9338 | 11120 |
| + sketch hash in 16-bit halves | 2296 | 2749 | 9087 | 10798 |
| + integer `order` keys | 1827 | 2144 | 7146 | 8697 |
| + one struct update per hit | 1755 | 2070 | 6891 | 8742 |

`bench/results/gap-m211b.exs` (`GAP_BASE=HEAD` compiles the parent's
`Policy` and `Sketch` from `git show` and runs them through the
`policy_module` hook, so before and after share one VM): five
paused/running pairs per n, one reader pinned to scheduler 1, four
schedulers. Duty, ns per read and placement come from a pass telemetry
handler. Both n=1000000 rows come from a second run of that size alone. In the
first run the "before" rounds were still settling (gaps 279, 206, 157,
118, 80), and its "after" median was 39 (29–46).

| n | tree | paused ns | running ns | gap median (range) | pass duty | ns per drained read | passes on the reader's scheduler |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 100000 | before | 362–370 | 404–414 | 42 (35–51) | 8.3–8.6% | 2580–2690 | 2036/2036 |
| 100000 | after | 365–374 | 404–413 | 38 (30–47) | 6.0–6.3% | 1875–1950 | 2034/2034 |
| 1000000 | before | 391–401 | 437–453 | 54 (39–57) | 8.8–10.0% | 2751–3187 | 2202/2202 |
| 1000000 | after | 394–400 | 432–524 | 37 (32–127) | 6.8–9.0% | 2139–2841 | 2255/2255 |

The first probe (`bench/results/footprint-m211b.exs`, `+S 2`) separated
the parts on the parent's tree at n=100000. A worker pinned to scheduler
2 drained the real ring once per millisecond into a replica policy. The
reader paid 3–16 ns over none for every variant, the full `on_access`
included, while the real process cost it 31–51 ns with 306/415 and then
424/424 of its passes on scheduler 1. In isolation, per drained read: the
`ids` lookup 163 ns, plus the `policy` lookup 330, plus the `order` lookup
769, the sketch increment alone 551, and the full `on_access` 2553.

With the old cap, the slowest 1% of passes (28) averaged 12141 µs, of
which the flush was 12134 µs (1009 events, no reads). Flushed events cost
6.3 µs each, drained reads 3.6 µs. At `max_size` 10_000 (5 s runs) the
old cap gave p99 11086 and max 13074. The cap of 256 gave p99 3362 and
max 6613. At that size one age chunk covers the whole 16_384-word sketch
(age max 2758 µs), and the slowest drain took 4297 µs.

Both A5 checks pass with the prototype (the worst sample is exactly the

### M2.8d — compiled probe of the bounded hit residue, tree of 847f900 (2026-10-28, no `mix bench get`: `lib/` unchanged, M2.8c's table is this tree's)

`bench/results/residue-m28d.exs` (gitignored), run with
`ERL_FLAGS="+sbt db +sbwt very_long"`: per n, three real caches populated as
the bench populates them, 2 M hits per row over the bench's Zipf(1.0) key
sample, one reader pinned to scheduler 1, then four readers pinned one per
scheduler; ns per hit, and the difference to the unbounded row. The replica
rows reimplement the hit path in the probe module step by step against the
bounded cache's table and their own atomics array, with a drainer process on
scheduler 3 that reads the stripe and writes `head` once per millisecond.
Layouts: A = `[tail head slots]` (the spec's `2 + slots`), B = `[tail pad7
head pad7 slots]` (`16 + slots`, the plan's first lever), C = `[slots tail
pad7 head pad7]` (`slots + 16`), A32 = A with 32 slots, "half" = the slot
written on even sequences only.

| row | n=1000, 1 reader | n=100000, 1 reader | n=1000000, 1 reader | n=1000, 4 readers | n=100000, 4 readers | n=1000000, 4 readers |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| unbounded get | 279 | 318 | 349 | 466 | 505 | 535 |
| bounded get, maintenance running | 346 (+67) | 411 (+93) | 447 (+98) | 617 (+151) | 637 (+132) | 674 (+139) |
| bounded get, maintenance paused | 327 (+48) | 369 (+51) | 396 (+47) | 555 (+89) | 592 (+87) | 626 (+91) |
| replica lookup+pt | 251 | 289 | 320 | 384 | 435 | 459 |
| replica +sched+add_get | 274 | 319 | 347 | 452 | 492 | 525 |
| replica +put A | 299 | 341 | 371 | 518 | 557 | 587 |
| replica +put A, drainer | 300 | 346 | 431 | 536 | 564 | 596 |
| replica +put B, drainer | 301 | 346 | 430 | 533 | 566 | 594 |
| replica +put C, drainer | 299 | 343 | 431 | 520 | 556 | 587 |
| replica +put A32, drainer | 299 | 344 | 431 | 528 | 565 | 599 |
| replica +put A half, drainer | 309 | 342 | 428 | 538 | 540 | 580 |
| replica +put C half, drainer | 297 | 349 | 434 | 501 | 536 | 572 |

The paused rows are the hit path alone: flat in n at one reader (the two
ops, 23–30 ns for the claim and 22–25 for the slot write in the replica)
and about 90 ns at four readers, where each op costs about 65 ns because a
locked read-modify-write stalls the thread while its SMT sibling runs. The
running-minus-paused difference (19, 42, 51 ns at one reader) is the
maintenance process. The replica drainer at n=1000000 one reader (+60 ns
over no drainer, against +1–5 at the smaller n) is the one row where
bouncing the stripe shows, with the whole L3 thrashed by the table; the
real caches' paused row at that n does not show it (the flag stays set, no
passes), and the layout does not change it (A, B, C, A32 all 430–431).

The real-cache rows again with a `drain_backoff: 7` cache, one reader, at
four schedulers and at two (`+S 2 +sbt db`, the schedulers on separate
physical cores), ns per hit and the difference to unbounded:

| row | n=1000, +S 4 | n=100000, +S 4 | n=1000000, +S 4 | n=1000, +S 2 | n=100000, +S 2 | n=1000000, +S 2 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| unbounded get | 267 | 310 | 353 | 267 | 312 | 343 |
| bounded get, maintenance running | 349 (+82) | 412 (+102) | 444 (+91) | 332 (+65) | 414 (+102) | 445 (+102) |
| bounded get, maintenance paused | 323 (+56) | 362 (+52) | 397 (+44) | 321 (+54) | 376 (+64) | 410 (+67) |
| bounded get, drain_backoff 7 | 339 (+72) | 390 (+80) | 430 (+77) | 337 (+70) | 383 (+71) | 426 (+83) |
| unbounded get (repeat) | 266 | 310 | 341 | 267 | 313 | 349 |

With the maintenance process on the other core the running-minus-paused
gap is 11, 38 and 35 ns, so the sibling accounts for little of it at
n ≥ 100000; `drain_backoff` 7 takes it to 16, 7 and 16 ns there and to
16, 28 and 33 ns at four schedulers. CPU: Intel i5-5257U, 2 cores × 2
threads, 3 MB L3 (`/proc/cpuinfo`; `erlang:system_info(cpu_topology)`
pairs logical 0 with 2 and 1 with 3).

### M2.8c — `mix bench get`, drain-pass duty cycle capped, tree of commit after fd918a2 (2026-10-28, 51/60 passed: 30 of 30 `theine_unbounded` checks, 21 of 30 `theine` checks)

Maintenance task that also ran the full `mix bench get` with
`ERL_FLAGS="+sbt db +sbwt very_long"`, because its gate is the bounded
A1/A2 figures. Before the bench, a compiled probe (`bench/results/duty-m28c.exs`,
gitignored: four readers pinned one per scheduler, Zipf(1.0) over 1_000
keys, 3 s per configuration, the pass telemetry summed) measured what the
maintenance process costs the readers at full parallelism for several
`drain_backoff` values on this tree:

| config | reads/s | passes/s | duty % of one core | mean pass µs | reads sampled/s |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| unbounded | 8_066_731 | 1 | 0.0 | 3.7 | 0 |
| bounded, drain_backoff 0 | 5_773_899 | 602 | 98.7 | 1641 | 153_945 |
| bounded, drain_backoff 3 (default) | 6_544_614 | 132 | 21.0 | 1586 | 33_796 |

| bounded, drain_backoff 3 (repeat) | 6_519_175 | 135 | 20.9 | 1547 | 34_642 |
| unbounded (repeat) | 8_050_375 | 1 | 0.0 | 3.3 | 0 |

So the cap does what it was for (99% to 21% busy, +13% reads/s), `k` 7
adds about 1% more, and the bounded subject at 0.81× the unbounded one is
the hit path's cost at this parallelism, not maintenance. The 1.6 ms mean
pass for 256 reads is wall time on a scheduler shared with a reader (with
one reader the same work took 110 µs per 64 reads, M2.8b); the deferral
after such a pass is about 5 ms, so the millisecond rounding of the timer
does not bite here, while at parallel 1 (passes of about 100 µs) it makes
the gap about 1 ms, so the policy samples each stripe's newest 64 reads
roughly once per millisecond under a saturating reader.

In the bench the bounded p99 at parallel 1 moved from 1.56–2.32× to
1.05–1.57×: under 1.5× on four of six shapes, 1.56× at n=1000 tuple3 (the
discovered-issue-4 shape, where `ets` itself read 382 ns) and 1.89× at
n=100000 binary_1kb, where the same shape's `theine_nostats` row (the same
hit path) read 1.33× and M2.8b's `theine` row 1.56×, so that row is an
outlier of the run rather than the tree. A2 at parallel 4 passes on the
three binary_1kb shapes (0.71–0.72×) and fails on the three tuple3 shapes
(0.57–0.62×), where `theine_unbounded` is at 0.67–0.72× and bounded runs
at 0.86–0.90× of it; p50 at parallel 1 is 1.41–1.55×, over the line at
n=100000 tuple3 (1.55×) and at the outlier row. All 30 `theine_unbounded`
checks pass (p50 1.20–1.34×, p99 0.89–1.31×, ips 0.67–0.81×). The nine
failures are discovered issue 6(c) and M2.8d.

### M2.8b — `mix bench get`, two-op ring with power-of-two masks, tree of commit after c87c2fa (2026-10-28, 36/60 passed: 26 of 30 `theine_unbounded` checks, 10 of 30 `theine` checks)

Hot-path task, full run with `ERL_FLAGS="+sbt db +sbwt very_long"`. The
bounded hit path is now at the budget specs/01 §Decisions expected: in a
compiled loop `theine` costs 53–64 ns over `theine_unbounded` (77 ns with
the two-op protocol and `rem`, 105 ns in M2.8). In the bench the bounded
p50 at parallel 1 moved from 1.62–1.78× to 1.45–1.58× `ets`: under the
1.5× line on four of the six shapes (n=1000 both values, n=1000000 both
values) and at 1.55/1.58× on the two n=100000 shapes, where this run's
`ets` floor (274/291 ns) and `theine_unbounded` (1.37/1.26×, 375/366 ns
against 349/374 ns in M2.8's run) sit inside the ±25 ns wander of
discovered issue 4. The bounded p99 at parallel 1 fails on every shape
(1.56–2.32×) for a reason that is not the hit path: a per-call latency
distribution over 1 M hits of one key (`+sbt db +sbwt very_long`, one
reader) gives, in ns,

| cache | p50 | p90 | p99 | p99.9 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| unbounded | 290 | 296 | 369–394 | 701–1789 |
| bounded, defaults (64 slots, threshold 32) | 340 | 360 | 589–698 | 897–1837 |
| bounded, 256 slots, threshold 128 | 337 | 355 | 476–524 | 888–1353 |
| bounded, maintenance paused (triggers fail the compare_exchange, no passes) | 340 | 348 | 410–479 | 703–778 |

so with no passes running the bounded p99 is 70–140 ns over its p50,
inside the +164 ns the 1.5× line allows at n=1000 tuple3, and the 250–360 ns
excess with passes running is the pass frequency: with one saturating
reader the maintenance process drains every 64 reads, each trigger that
finds the flag clear pays a `send` that wakes the process on another
scheduler, and each pass reads the stripe's nine cache lines and writes
`head` on the reader's `tail` line, so the reader's next `add_get` and the
first `put` on each line pay a cross-core transfer. Quartering the pass
frequency (the 256/128 row) halves the excess. That is exactly what the
M2.8c duty-cycle cap changes (passes once per `k × d` instead of back to
back; triggers in between fail the compare_exchange), so the p99 part of
the parallel-1 gate moves to M2.8c with the A2 ips figure, and the defaults
stay at 64/32. The four `theine_unbounded` failures (p99 1.65× at n=1000
tuple3 parallel=1, the discovered-issue-4 shape; ips 0.63 and 0.65 and p50
1.53× at parallel 4 tuple3) are the ±0.1× wander noted in M1.5–M2.3: the
unbounded branch of `get/2` is unchanged by this commit (the `hit` match
fails at the tuple test either way), and M2.8's run had the same rows at
1.39–1.47× and 0.67–0.73×.

### M2.8 — `mix bench get`, bounded `theine` and `theine_nostats` added, tree of commit after 9c6c6bf (2026-10-28, 31/60 passed: all 30 `theine_unbounded` checks, 1 of 30 `theine` checks)

Hot-path task, full run with `ERL_FLAGS="+sbt db +sbwt very_long"`, about
16 minutes with four subjects. The gate for this commit is the
`theine_unbounded` set, which previously passed: 30/30 here (p50 1.23–1.47×,
p99 0.85–1.35×, ips 0.67–0.83× at parallel 4). The 29 failures are all the
new bounded subject, p50 1.62–1.86×, p99 1.49–2.44×, ips 0.38–0.52× at
parallel 4, for the two reasons in discovered issue 6 (M2.8b, M2.8c).
Earlier runs of the day on the same tree shape: 25/60 with the first
five-key `get/2` (unbounded p50 1.52–1.62× at parallel 4 tuple3, a
regression), 26/60 with the one-key inlined version (1.48–1.54×), and two
`--sizes 1000` comparisons: HEAD's `get/2` on today's machine 20/20
(unbounded 1.27/1.36× tuple3, 1.26/1.38× binary), this tree without the
bounded subjects 9/10 (1.30/1.43×, 1.31/1.39×; the failed check was ips
0.649 ≥ 0.65). The n=100000 tuple3 parallel=1 `ets` floor in the final run
(362 ns, against 271–272 in every other run today) is the noisy floor of
discovered issue 4 on a different shape; it made the unbounded row read
0.96× and did not decide any check.

### M2.3 — `mix bench get`, theine_unbounded and theine_nostats vs ets, tree of commit after be396e0 (2026-10-27, 29/30 passed in the full run, 10/10 in the `--sizes 1000` confirmation)

Regression check only: this commit adds `Theine.ReadBuffer` and moves the
tail sum out of `Theine.Stats.hits/1`, which `get/2` does not call. Run
with `ERL_FLAGS="+sbt db +sbwt very_long"`. p50 1.20–1.38×, p99 1.10–1.33×,
ips 0.66–0.86× of `ets` for `theine_unbounded`, the band of M1.5 and M1.6.
The one failed check is the usual one, `theine_unbounded` p99 at n=1000
tuple3 parallel=1 at 1.51× over an `ets` p99 floor of 362 ns, the lowest
floor in any log here (384–433 before); `theine_unbounded`'s own p99
there, 548 ns, is inside the 471–696 band of every earlier run. The
`--sizes 1000` rerun of the same tree passed 10/10 with the shape at 1.29×
(floor 403 ns), which clears it per specs/09 §Decisions M1.6. That rerun
showed `theine_nostats` p99 at 1.58× on n=1000 binary_1kb parallel=1 (670
vs 485 ns in the full run, p50 unchanged at 300–306 ns): no threshold
applies to that subject, and it is the same one-off p99 wobble on a
different shape.

### M1.6 — `mix bench get`, theine_unbounded and theine_nostats vs ets, tree of commit after f18387b (2026-10-27, 29/30 passed in the full run, 10/10 in the `--sizes 1000` confirmation)

The hit path gained one branch: `%{request_counters: nil}` skips the
counter op, `%{request_counters: c}` adds to it (one map key, like the
`%{counters: c}` match before). A first full run with a two-key match
(`%{stats: true, counters: c}`) was also 29/30 and read a few ns slower at
p50 on every single-scheduler shape, which is why the field exists
(specs/08 §Decisions M1.6). `theine_nostats` (unbounded, `stats: false`,
no threshold) is 1.13–1.23× `ets` at p50 and 0.82–0.90× at ips, 25–60 ns
under `theine_unbounded` at p50: the counter op plus the branch.

Both full runs failed one check, `theine_unbounded` p99 at n=1000 tuple3
parallel=1 (1.62×, then 1.64×). The `ets` p99 floor at that shape was 338
and 408 ns in those runs against 433 in the `--sizes 1000` rerun of the
same tree, where the shape passed at 1.29× and all ten n=1000 checks
passed; `theine_unbounded`'s own p99 there (548, 668, 558 ns) sits in the
band of every earlier log (471–696 ns). Treated as floor noise per
specs/09 §Decisions M1.6, written in this commit. Everything else: p50
1.22–1.36×, p99 1.10–1.31×, ips 0.68–0.85× of `ets`, as M1.5.

### M0.4b — `mix bench hitrate`, reference LRU on the ARC traces (2026-10-27)

| trace | size | policy | requests | hit rate % | seconds |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OLTP | 1000 | lru | 914145 | 32.83 | 9.27 |
| OLTP | 2000 | lru | 914145 | 42.47 | 9.27 |
| OLTP | 5000 | lru | 914145 | 53.65 | 9.27 |
| DS1 | 1000000 | lru | 43704979 | 3.09 | 249.35 |
| DS1 | 4000000 | lru | 43704979 | 20.24 | 249.35 |
| DS1 | 8000000 | lru | 43704979 | 43.03 | 249.35 |
| S3 | 100000 | lru | 16407702 | 2.33 | 160.03 |
| S3 | 400000 | lru | 16407702 | 12.04 | 160.03 |
| P3 | 50000 | lru | 3912296 | 6.86 | 5.60 |

These LRU figures equal the LRU column of Table 2 in the ARC paper
(Megiddo & Modha, FAST '03) to two decimals, which validates the `.lis`
parser, the block expansion and the reference LRU against the original
source. The W-TinyLFU rows added in M2.10 are compared against these. The
full ARC run takes about 7 minutes on the 4-core reference laptop, almost
all of it DS1 and S3; policy tasks that want a quick signal can use
`--traces OLTP,P3,loop,zipf` for iteration and run the full set before
committing.

M0.5 `lru` baseline (`mix bench hitrate --traces loop,zipf`, parent a06d881):

| trace | size | policy | requests | hit rate % |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| loop | 1000 | lru | 150000 | 0.00 |
| zipf | 10000 | lru | 10000000 | 56.61 |
| zipf | 100000 | lru | 10000000 | 76.41 |

## Final gate

M5.6a, 2026-11-03, on the tree of 7e8712e (`lib/` last changed in M5.3).
The development machine: Intel(R) Core(TM) i5-5257U CPU @ 2.70GHz;
Linux 7.2.5-3-omarchy; OTP 28; `schedulers_online` 4; Elixir 1.20.4. The
benches ran one at a time on a quiet machine with
`ERL_FLAGS="+sbt db +sbwt very_long"`, except `mix bench hitrate`, whose
replays are deterministic. The test suite ran beside its first half.

| step | result |
| --- | --- |
| `mix format --check-formatted` | clean |
| `mix compile --warnings-as-errors` | clean |
| `mix test --include stress --cover` | 410 passed (35 doctests, 13 properties, 362 tests), 0 failures, 72.7 s, seed 302928; `Theine.Loader` 100.00%, `Theine.Policy` 100.00%, total 98.11% |
| `MIX_ENV=test mix dialyzer` | 0 errors |
| `mix docs` | no warnings |
| `mix hex.build` (`--unpack --output bench/results/hex-m56`) | theine 0.1.0: `lib` (15 files), `mix.exs`, `README.md`, `LICENSE`; one dependency, `telemetry ~> 1.3`; MIT |
| `mix bench hitrate` | exit 0: 19/19 (`--traces OLTP,S3,P3,loop,zipf`, 44 min) and 6/6 (`--traces DS1`, 79 min) |
| `mix bench maintenance` | exit 0: 4/4 |
| `mix bench get --dev-floor` | exit 1 in each run: full run 72 pass, 17 floor, 1 fail; `--sizes 1000 --scenarios get` reruns 17/9/2 and 25/2/1. Every `fail` is A1 p99 at n=1000 parallel 1. M5.6b found no lever for it in specs/03 or 04: the row misses with maintenance paused too (§Benchmark log M5.6b, open question 4) |

| criterion | development machine | reference machine |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A1 latency | `pass` or `floor` on every row except p99 at n=1000 parallel 1: `fail` in all three runs (tuple3 2.02, 1.91, 1.67; binary_1kb 1.51, 1.92, 1.46), deferred to the reference machine (open question 4, specs/09 §Decisions 2026-11-03) | human gate pending, this row included |
| A2 throughput | `floor` (full run; the reruns' n=1000 rows were 0.57–0.59 and 1.91–1.93): ips/ets 0.59–0.63 on tuple3 at parallel 4 (binary_1kb 0.69–0.73 pass); ips/cachex 1.81–1.94 (bounded) and 1.98 (unbounded, n=1000000 binary_1kb); ips/nebulex 5.24–6.20, pass | human gate pending |
| put burst | pass on all six shapes, 5.58–7.36× an ETS insert (limit 10) | human gate pending |
| A3 hit rate | `theine ≥ lru` on 12/12 rows, `loop` +58.67 points (limit +10), `theine ≥ theine_static − 1` on 12/12 (worst −0.71, P3) | Caffeine simulator: human gate pending |
| A4 single-flight | pass: the stress tier's exactly-once loader soak and the loader suite | — |
| A5 bounded | pass: worst sample 1_001_028 = limit, 1_000_000 after 1 s | — |
| A6 no native code | pass: test/theine/package_test.exs and the package listing above | — |

### `mix bench hitrate`

`--traces DS1` (exit 0, 6/6, four replay passes). Default flags, 32
accesses per pass. Every hit count matches M2.13's (`hitrate-7bbe3b6.json`,

matches M2.13's table to the hundredth:

### `mix bench maintenance`

Exit 0, 4/4, `max_size` 1_000_000, four writers, `ERL_FLAGS` as above:

| max sample (limit 1001028) | samples | writes (write run) | settled size | passes | p50 us | p99 us | max us | reads (mixed) | writes (mixed) | call-path writes | fraction |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1001028 | 2626 | 2879496 | 1000000 | 8714 | 2065.47 | 3958.48 | 11703.85 | 11824992 | 3940378 | 12407 | 0.00 |

The pass p99 is 3.96 ms against the 5 ms `pass_budget` (M3.1 had
3.99–4.17, M3.2 3.74; discovered issue 10), and the max, 11.7 ms, is
under 4× the budget. The write run's distinct-key puts, each of which
evicts, ran at about 96_000/s for the whole cache.

### Reference machine run (A1/A2, specs/09 §Reference machine)
